| `5-6-54` | MAY | A server may buffer a limited number of 0-RTT packets awaiting a late Initial packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MAY buffer a limited number of these packets in anticipation of a late-arriving Initial packet. | N/A — server-side, and 0-RTT is a project non-goal. |
| `5-6-55` | SHOULD | Clients cannot send Handshake packets before receiving a server response, so servers should ignore any that arrive; the client premise constrains the client send path. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | Clients are not able to send Handshake packets prior to receiving a server response, so servers SHOULD ignore any such packets. | quic/handshake.go + conn_seal.go — handshake keys must only exist after the server flight, structurally preventing early Handshake-packet sends; confirm no path emits Handshake-level packets pre-keys. |
| `5-6-56` | MUST | Servers must drop incoming packets in all circumstances not explicitly handled above. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers MUST drop incoming packets under all other circumstances. | N/A server-side. |
| `5-6-57` | SHOULD | A server deployment with address-based load balancing and no continuity solution should advertise disable_active_migration. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | SHOULD indicate that migration is not supported by using the disable_active_migration transport parameter. | quic/transport_params.go — client must at least parse/tolerate disable_active_migration; client never migrates, so nothing further to enforce. |
| `5-6-58` | informative | disable_active_migration does not forbid migration performed after the client acts on a preferred_address transport parameter. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The disable_active_migration transport parameter does not prohibit connection migration after a client has acted on a preferred_address transport parameter. | N/A — client neither migrates nor uses preferred_address. |
| `5-6-59` | MUST | Server deployments using simple address-based load balancing must avoid creating a stateless reset oracle. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Server deployments that use this simple form of load balancing MUST avoid the creation of a stateless reset oracle | N/A server-side deployment concern. |
| `5-6-60` | informative | A QUIC implementation must expose the §5.3 operations to the application protocol; client role: open a connection, enable Early Data, and learn Early Data accept/reject. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | An application protocol can assume that an implementation of QUIC provides an interface that includes the operations described in this section. | quic/conn.go public API — http3.Dial covers open; Early Data operations intentionally absent (0-RTT is a project non-goal, only-those-operations-used caveat applies). |
| `5-6-61` | informative | Either role must be able to: configure initial stream limits, set stream/connection flow-control limits, query handshake completion, keep the connection alive (e.g., PING) before idle timeout, and immediately close. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | keep a connection from silently closing, by either generating PING frames (Section 19.2) or requesting that the transport send additional frames before the idle timeout expires | quic/conn.go + transport_params.go — verify configurable initial stream/flow limits, a keepalive/PING mechanism against idle timeout, handshake-complete signal, and an immediate-close API exist. |
| `5-6-62` | SHOULD | Clients supporting multiple QUIC versions should pad their first UDP datagram (with PADDING frames) to the largest of the minimum datagram sizes across all supported versions. <br>_(client,sender [CLIENT])_ | Clients that support multiple QUIC versions SHOULD ensure that the first UDP datagram they send is sized to the largest of the minimum datagram sizes from all versions they support | quic/initial.go + packet_write.go — client is v1-only, but the first datagram must still meet v1's 1200-byte minimum (§14.1); verify Initial padding. |
| `5-6-63` | informative | A Version Negotiation packet contains the list of versions the server will accept. <br>_(server,client [CLIENT])_ | This includes a list of versions that the server will accept. | quic/packet.go — VN payload parses as a sequence of 32-bit version values (needed for the 5-6-68 check). |
| `5-6-64` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must never send a Version Negotiation packet in response to receiving a Version Negotiation packet. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT send a Version Negotiation packet in response to receiving a Version Negotiation packet. | quic/conn_recv.go — trivially satisfied if the client has no VN-send path; confirm none exists. |
| `5-6-65` | MAY | A server may limit the total number of Version Negotiation packets it sends (e.g., not answering recognizable 0-RTT). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY limit the number of Version Negotiation packets it sends. | N/A server-side. |
| `5-6-66` | MUST | A client supporting only QUIC v1 must abandon the connection attempt upon receiving a Version Negotiation packet, subject to the two discard exceptions. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client that supports only this version of QUIC MUST abandon the current connection attempt if it receives a Version Negotiation packet, with the following two exceptions. | quic/conn_recv.go — on a valid VN packet, abort the handshake and surface a version-negotiation error to the Dial caller. |
| `5-6-67` | MUST | A client must discard any Version Negotiation packet once it has received and successfully processed any other packet (including an earlier VN packet). <br>_(client,receiver [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST discard any Version Negotiation packet if it has received and successfully processed any other packet, including an earlier Version Negotiation packet. | quic/conn_recv.go — need a processed-any-packet flag; after the first successfully processed server packet, VN packets are ignored (prevents late/injected VN killing a live handshake). |
| `5-6-68` | MUST | A client must discard a Version Negotiation packet whose version list contains the version the client selected. <br>_(client,receiver [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST discard a Version Negotiation packet that lists the QUIC version selected by the client. | quic/conn_recv.go — scan the VN version list; if 0x00000001 is present, drop the VN packet instead of abandoning (downgrade-injection defense). |
| `5-6-69` | informative | Version numbers matching the pattern 0x?a?a?a?a are reserved for greasing fields that carry version numbers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Some version numbers (0x?a?a?a?a, as defined in Section 15) are reserved for inclusion in fields that contain version numbers. | quic/conn_recv.go + packet.go — greased 0x?a?a?a?a entries in a VN list are ordinary unsupported versions; parser must not choke or treat them as v1. |
| `5-6-70` | MAY | Endpoints may insert reserved versions into any field where unknown versions are ignored, to test that peers ignore them. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY add reserved versions to any field where unknown or unsupported versions are ignored to test that a peer correctly ignores the value. | quic/packet_write.go — optional greasing on send; not implementing it is conforming, but receive path must tolerate it (5-6-69). |
| `5-6-71` | MAY | Endpoints may send whole packets with a reserved version to test that the peer correctly discards them. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY send packets with a reserved version to test that a peer correctly discards the packet. | quic/conn_recv.go — receive side must silently discard reserved-version packets (falls out of 5-6-47); sending grease packets is optional and unimplemented. |

### 9000 section 7 - Cryptographic and Transport Handshake (RFC 9000, Sections 7-7.5)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `7-1` | MUST | The cryptographic handshake must provide authenticated key exchange in which the server is always authenticated, the client optionally, every connection yields distinct unrelated keys, and keying material covers 0-RTT and 1-RTT packet protection. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The cryptographic handshake MUST provide the following properties | quic/handshake.go + crypto*.go: verify TLS 1.3 server certificate verification is enforced and per-connection secrets drive packet protection keys. |
| `7-2` | MUST | The handshake must provide authenticated exchange of both endpoints' transport parameters, with confidentiality protection for the server's transport parameters. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | authenticated exchange of values for transport parameters of both endpoints, and confidentiality protection for server transport parameters | quic/handshake.go + transport_params.go: TPs carried in the TLS extension; server TPs arrive in EncryptedExtensions (Handshake-level protected). |
| `7-3` | MUST | The handshake must provide authenticated negotiation of an application protocol (ALPN in TLS). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | authenticated negotiation of an application protocol | quic/handshake.go: ALPN offered in ClientHello and the negotiated protocol taken from the authenticated TLS handshake (http3 sets "h3"). |
| `7-4` | informative | The QUIC version defined by this document is identified by version number 0x00000001 and uses TLS as its cryptographic handshake. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The version of QUIC defined in this document is identified as 0x00000001 and uses TLS as described in [QUIC-TLS] | quic/packet.go / initial.go: version constant must be 0x00000001 in long headers and version-specific key derivation. |
| `7-5` | informative | CRYPTO frames may appear in different packet number spaces, and the CRYPTO stream offset starts from zero independently in each packet number space. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The offsets used by CRYPTO frames to ensure ordered delivery of cryptographic handshake data start from zero in each packet number space. | quic/conn_crypto.go: per-encryption-level CRYPTO reassembly cursors must each begin at offset 0 (Initial, Handshake, 1-RTT tracked separately). |
| `7-6` | MUST | Endpoints must explicitly negotiate an application protocol; there must be no implicit protocol agreement. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MUST explicitly negotiate an application protocol. | quic/handshake.go: handshake must fail if the server selects no ALPN protocol; check http3.Dial requires "h3" to be negotiated. |
| `7-7` | informative | An endpoint validates ECN support by observing whether ACK frames acknowledging its first packets carry ECN counts (per Section 13.4.2). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint validates support for ECN by observing whether the ACK frames acknowledging the first packets it sends carry ECN counts | **VERIFY** quic/ack_tracker.go: ECN is optional; confirm the client neither sets ECT codepoints nor claims ECN validation (N/A if unimplemented). -- Quote drops the article "the" before "ACK frames". Source (lines 1760-1762) reads: "An endpoint validates support for ECN by observing whether the ACK frames acknowledging the first packets it sends carry ECN counts, as described in Section 13.4.2." Fix the quote to "An endpoint validates support for ECN by observing whether the ACK frames acknowledging the first packets it sends carry ECN counts". Level (informative - no RFC 2119 keyword) and applies_to (endpoint / client_relevant true) are correct. |
| `7-8` | informative | The cryptographic handshake data is carried in Initial and Handshake packets. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The cryptographic handshake is carried in Initial (Section 17.2.2) and Handshake (Section 17.2.4) packets. | quic/conn_recv.go + conn_seal.go: CRYPTO frames sent/processed at Initial and Handshake encryption levels during the handshake. |
| `7-9` | informative | Multiple QUIC packets, even of different packet types, can be coalesced into a single UDP datagram (per Section 12.2). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Multiple QUIC packets -- even of different packet types -- can be coalesced into a single UDP datagram | quic/packet_write.go (send) + conn_recv.go (receive): support coalesced Initial+Handshake+1-RTT datagrams both ways. |
| `7-10` | informative | The server acknowledges 0-RTT data with ACKs sent in 1-RTT packets because 0-RTT and 1-RTT share the application-data packet number space. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | the server acknowledges 0-RTT data in 1-RTT packets, and the client sends 1-RTT packets in the same packet number space | quic/ack_tracker.go + loss.go: single ApplicationData PN space; no 0-RTT sent by this client, so only 1-RTT uses it. |
| `7-11` | informative | After processing the first Initial packet from the peer, an endpoint sets the Destination Connection ID of subsequent packets to the Source Connection ID it received. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | each endpoint sets the Destination Connection ID field in subsequent packets it sends to the value of the Source Connection ID field that it received | quic/conn.go + connid.go: on the first server Initial, adopt the server's SCID as our DCID for all later packets. |
| `7-12` | MUST | The client's initial Destination Connection ID (before any server Initial or Retry is received) must be an unpredictable value at least 8 bytes long. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | This Destination Connection ID MUST be at least 8 bytes in length. | quic/initial.go / connid.go: verify the random original DCID is generated with length >= 8 from a CSPRNG. |
| `7-13` | MUST | Until any packet is received from the server, the client must use the same Destination Connection ID value on every packet of the connection. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Until a packet is received from the server, the client MUST use the same Destination Connection ID value on all packets in this connection. | quic/conn.go + packet_write.go: DCID must not change across Initial retransmissions/PTO probes before the first server packet arrives. |
| `7-14` | informative | Initial packet protection keys are derived from the Destination Connection ID field of the client's first Initial packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The Destination Connection ID field from the first Initial packet sent by a client is used to determine packet protection keys for Initial packets. | quic/initial.go: Initial secrets HKDF-derived from the original DCID, not from any later DCID. |
| `7-15` | informative | Initial packet protection keys change after a Retry packet is received (re-derived from the Retry's Source Connection ID). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | These keys change after receiving a Retry packet; see Section 5.2 of [QUIC-TLS]. | quic/retry.go + initial.go: after Retry, re-derive Initial keys from the new DCID (the Retry SCID) before resending the Initial. |
| `7-16` | informative | The client populates the Source Connection ID field with a value of its own choosing and sets the Source Connection ID Length field accordingly. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client populates the Source Connection ID field with a value of its choosing and sets the Source Connection ID Length field to indicate the length. | quic/initial.go + packet_write.go: client SCID chosen locally; SCID Length field must match the actual SCID byte length. |
| `7-17` | informative | 0-RTT packets in the first flight use the same Destination and Source Connection ID values as the client's first Initial packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | 0-RTT packets in the first flight use the same Destination Connection ID and Source Connection ID values as the client's first Initial packet. | quic/: 0-RTT not implemented — confirm no 0-RTT packet emission path exists; rule is vacuous. |
| `7-18` | informative | Upon first receiving an Initial or Retry packet from the server, the client uses the server-supplied Source Connection ID as the Destination Connection ID for subsequent packets. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client uses the Source Connection ID supplied by the server as the Destination Connection ID for subsequent packets, including any 0-RTT packets | quic/conn.go + retry.go: DCID switch happens on the first server Initial and (if present) once on Retry. |
| `7-19` | MUST | Once the client has received a valid Initial packet from the server, it must discard any subsequent packet on that connection carrying a different Source Connection ID. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Once a client has received a valid Initial packet from the server, it MUST discard any subsequent packet it receives on that connection with a different Source Connection ID. | quic/conn_recv.go: after the first valid server Initial, compare SCID of later long-header packets against the recorded server SCID and drop mismatches. |
| `7-20` | MUST | The client must change the Destination Connection ID it sends in response to only the first received Initial or Retry packet, never in response to later ones. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST change the Destination Connection ID it uses for sending packets in response to only the first received Initial or Retry packet. | quic/conn.go + retry.go: DCID may be updated at most once for a Retry and once for the first server Initial; ignore SCIDs of later packets. |
| `7-21` | MUST | The server must set the Destination Connection ID it uses for sending based on the first received Initial packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST set the Destination Connection ID it uses for sending packets based on the first received Initial packet. | Server-side rule; only relevant to the in-repo test peer if it implements a server role. |
| `7-22` | informative | After the handshake fixes connection IDs, any further change to the Destination Connection ID is permitted only using values taken from NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Any further changes to the Destination Connection ID are only permitted if the values are taken from NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames | quic/connid.go: with no migration, the client should never switch DCID post-handshake except via stored NEW_CONNECTION_ID values; verify no other mutation path. |
| `7-23` | MUST | If subsequent Initial packets include a different Source Connection ID than the first, they must be discarded. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | if subsequent Initial packets include a different Source Connection ID, they MUST be discarded | quic/conn_recv.go: Initial packets arriving after the first with a changed SCID are dropped, not processed (overlaps 7-19 check). |
| `7-24` | informative | Each endpoint includes the Source Connection ID from the first Initial packet it sent in its initial_source_connection_id transport parameter. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Each endpoint includes the value of the Source Connection ID field from the first Initial packet it sent in the initial_source_connection_id transport parameter | quic/transport_params.go: client must emit initial_source_connection_id equal to its first Initial's SCID. |
| `7-25` | informative | The server includes the Destination Connection ID from the first client Initial it received (pre-Retry, if a Retry was sent) in original_destination_connection_id. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server includes the Destination Connection ID field from the first Initial packet it received from the client in the original_destination_connection_id transport parameter | quic/transport_params.go + handshake.go: client validates the server's original_destination_connection_id against the random DCID of its own first Initial. |
| `7-26` | informative | If the server sent a Retry packet, it also includes the Retry packet's Source Connection ID in the retry_source_connection_id transport parameter. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | If it sends a Retry packet, a server also includes the Source Connection ID field from the Retry packet in the retry_source_connection_id transport parameter. | quic/retry.go + transport_params.go: client validates retry_source_connection_id against the SCID of the received Retry packet. |
| `7-27` | MUST | Peer-provided values of the connection-ID transport parameters must match the values the endpoint actually used in the Destination and Source Connection ID fields of its Initial packets. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The values provided by a peer for these transport parameters MUST match the values that an endpoint used in the Destination and Source Connection ID fields of Initial packets | quic/transport_params.go: cross-check initial_source_connection_id / original_destination_connection_id / retry_source_connection_id against recorded wire CIDs. |
| `7-28` | MUST | Endpoints must validate that received transport parameters match the connection ID values received on the wire (anti-injection authentication of handshake CIDs). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MUST validate that received transport parameters match received connection ID values. | quic/handshake.go: verify a post-TP-receipt validation step exists comparing server SCID (and Retry SCID) to the authenticated TP values. |
| `7-29` | MUST | Absence of initial_source_connection_id from either endpoint, or absence of original_destination_connection_id from the server, must be treated as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | MUST treat the absence of the initial_source_connection_id transport parameter from either endpoint or the absence of the original_destination_connection_id transport parameter from the server as a connection error | quic/transport_params.go + errors.go: missing mandatory CID params -> close with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR (0x8). |
| `7-30` | MUST | Absence of retry_source_connection_id from the server after the client received a Retry packet must be treated as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST treat the following as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION: absence of the retry_source_connection_id transport parameter from the server after receiving a Retry packet | quic/retry.go + transport_params.go: if a Retry was processed, require retry_source_connection_id to be present or close the connection. |
| `7-31` | MUST | Presence of the retry_source_connection_id transport parameter when no Retry packet was received must be treated as a connection error (TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | presence of the retry_source_connection_id transport parameter when no Retry packet was received | quic/transport_params.go: reject retry_source_connection_id when the connection saw no Retry. |
| `7-32` | MUST | A mismatch between the connection-ID transport parameter values received from the peer and the corresponding Destination/Source Connection ID fields of Initial packets must be treated as a connection error (TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | a mismatch between values received from a peer in these transport parameters and the value sent in the corresponding Destination or Source Connection ID fields of Initial packets | quic/transport_params.go: byte-compare each CID TP against the wire value; mismatch -> connection close with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. |
| `7-33` | informative | If a zero-length connection ID is selected, the corresponding connection-ID transport parameter is still included, with a zero-length value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If a zero-length connection ID is selected, the corresponding transport parameter is included with a zero-length value. | quic/transport_params.go: encoder/decoder must handle zero-length CID TP values (present with empty payload, not omitted). |
| `7-34` | informative | Endpoints are required to comply with the restrictions that each transport parameter's definition imposes, and each endpoint validates the values its peer provides. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints are required to comply with the restrictions that each parameter defines | quic/transport_params.go: per-parameter range/validity checks exist for every parameter defined in Section 18.2. |
| `7-35` | MUST | Receipt of a transport parameter with an invalid value must be treated as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST treat receipt of a transport parameter with an invalid value as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. | quic/transport_params.go + errors.go: out-of-range values (e.g. ack_delay_exponent>20, max_udp_payload_size<1200) -> TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. |
| `7-36` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not send the same transport parameter more than once within a transport parameters extension. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT send a parameter more than once in a given transport parameters extension. | quic/transport_params.go: encoder must emit each parameter ID at most once. |
| `7-37` | SHOULD | Receipt of duplicate transport parameters should be treated as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint SHOULD treat receipt of duplicate transport parameters as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. | quic/transport_params.go: decoder tracks seen IDs and rejects duplicates with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. |
| `7-38` | informative | The transport parameters a client sends during the handshake apply to all application protocols it offers via ALPN. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The transport parameters that a client includes during the handshake apply to all application protocols that the client offers. | quic/handshake.go: single TP set per connection regardless of ALPN list (this client offers only "h3"; trivially satisfied). |
| `7-39` | MUST | Any definition of a new transport parameter must specify whether storing it for 0-RTT is mandatory, optional, or prohibited. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The definition of a new transport parameter (Section 7.4.2) MUST specify whether storing the transport parameter for 0-RTT is mandatory, optional, or prohibited. | Spec-authoring rule; N/A to quic/ code (this client defines no new transport parameters). |
| `7-40` | informative | A client need not store, for 0-RTT purposes, a transport parameter it cannot process. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client need not store a transport parameter it cannot process. | N/A: no 0-RTT in quic/; no TP storage for resumption exists. |
| `7-41` | MUST NOT | A client must not use remembered values for ack_delay_exponent, max_ack_delay, initial_source_connection_id, original_destination_connection_id, preferred_address, retry_source_connection_id, or stateless_reset_token when attempting 0-RTT. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT use remembered values for the following parameters: ack_delay_exponent, max_ack_delay, initial_source_connection_id, original_destination_connection_id, preferred_address, retry_source_connection_id, and stateless_reset_token. | quic/: 0-RTT unimplemented — rule vacuous; if 0-RTT is ever added, transport_params.go must exclude these seven from the remembered set. |
| `7-42` | MUST | For those non-remembered parameters the client must use the server's newly supplied handshake values, falling back to defaults if the server provides none. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client MUST use the server's new values in the handshake instead; if the server does not provide new values, the default values are used. | quic/transport_params.go: defaults (ack_delay_exponent=3, max_ack_delay=25ms, etc.) applied when a parameter is absent from the server's TPs. |
| `7-43` | MUST | A client that attempts to send 0-RTT data must remember all other server transport parameters that it is able to process. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client that attempts to send 0-RTT data MUST remember all other transport parameters used by the server that it is able to process. | quic/: vacuous — client never attempts 0-RTT; confirm no session-ticket TP persistence claims 0-RTT capability. |
| `7-44` | MUST NOT | A server that accepts 0-RTT data must not reduce any limits or alter any values that the client's 0-RTT data might then violate. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the server MUST NOT reduce any limits or alter any values that might be violated by the client with its 0-RTT data | Server-side 0-RTT rule; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-45` | MUST NOT | A server accepting 0-RTT must not set active_connection_id_limit, initial_max_data, initial_max_stream_data_bidi_local, initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote, initial_max_stream_data_uni, initial_max_streams_bidi, or initial_max_streams_uni smaller than the remembered values. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | a server that accepts 0-RTT data MUST NOT set values for the following parameters (Section 18.2) that are smaller than the remembered values of the parameters | Server-side 0-RTT rule; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-46` | SHOULD | The subset of transport parameters that permit sending application data (initial_max_data plus a streams/stream-data pair) should be set to non-zero values when enabling 0-RTT. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | The applicable subset of transport parameters that permit the sending of application data SHOULD be set to non-zero values for 0-RTT. | Server-side 0-RTT advisory; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-47` | informative | Once the handshake completes, the client updates flow control limits on all its sending streams using the handshake's initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote and initial_max_stream_data_uni values. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client updates the flow control limits on all sending streams using the updated values of initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote and initial_max_stream_data_uni | quic/stream.go + transport_params.go: send-side stream limits initialized from the server's handshake TPs (no remembered-value phase since no 0-RTT). |
| `7-48` | MAY | A server may store and recover previously sent max_idle_timeout, max_udp_payload_size, and disable_active_migration values and reject 0-RTT if it now selects smaller values. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY store and recover the previously sent values of the max_idle_timeout, max_udp_payload_size, and disable_active_migration parameters and reject 0-RTT if it selects smaller values. | Server-side 0-RTT rule; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-49` | MUST | A server must reject 0-RTT data if the restored transport parameter values cannot be supported. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST reject 0-RTT data if the restored values for transport parameters cannot be supported. | Server-side 0-RTT rule; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-50` | MUST | When sending frames in 0-RTT packets, a client must use only the remembered transport parameters. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | When sending frames in 0-RTT packets, a client MUST only use remembered transport parameters | quic/: vacuous — no 0-RTT send path; keep it that way or gate on remembered TPs if added. |
| `7-51` | MUST NOT | In 0-RTT packets a client must not use updated values learned from the server's new transport parameters or from frames received in 1-RTT packets; updated values apply only to 1-RTT packets. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | it MUST NOT use updated values that it learns from the server's updated transport parameters or from frames received in 1-RTT packets | quic/: vacuous without 0-RTT; note for any future 0-RTT work in conn.go/stream.go limit accounting. |
| `7-52` | MAY | A server may treat a client's use of updated transport parameter values in 0-RTT as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY treat the use of updated transport parameters in 0-RTT as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. | Server-side 0-RTT enforcement; N/A to client-only quic/. |
| `7-53` | MUST | An endpoint must ignore transport parameters it does not support; absence of a parameter disables the optional feature it negotiates. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST ignore transport parameters that it does not support. | quic/transport_params.go: decoder must skip unknown parameter IDs (read id+length, skip value) without error. |
| `7-54` | informative | Some transport parameter identifiers are reserved specifically to exercise the ignore-unknown-parameters requirement (GREASE, per Section 18.1). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | some identifiers are reserved in order to exercise this requirement | quic/transport_params.go: reserved IDs (31*N+27) from servers must be silently ignored; optionally emit one for GREASE. |
| `7-55` | MUST | Implementations must support buffering at least 4096 bytes of data received in out-of-order CRYPTO frames. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Implementations MUST support buffering at least 4096 bytes of data received in out-of-order CRYPTO frames. | quic/conn_crypto.go: out-of-order CRYPTO reassembly buffer capacity must be >= 4096 bytes per the buffering policy. |
| `7-56` | MAY | Endpoints may allow more than 4096 bytes of CRYPTO data to be buffered during the handshake (useful for large keys or credentials), and the buffer size need not stay constant. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY choose to allow more data to be buffered during the handshake. | quic/conn_crypto.go: check the handshake-phase buffer cap is generous enough for real certificate chains (>4 KiB typical). |
| `7-57` | MUST | If an endpoint's CRYPTO buffer is exceeded during the handshake and it does not expand the buffer, it must close the connection with a CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED error code. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If an endpoint does not expand its buffer, it MUST close the connection with a CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED error code. | quic/conn_crypto.go + errors.go: on handshake-phase buffer overflow, close with CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED (0x0d), not silent drop. |
| `7-58` | MAY | After the handshake completes, an endpoint unable to buffer CRYPTO data may either discard that and all future CRYPTO frames, or close the connection with CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | it MAY discard that CRYPTO frame and all CRYPTO frames received in the future, or it MAY close the connection with a CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED error code | quic/conn_crypto.go: post-handshake CRYPTO (e.g. NewSessionTicket) handling — buffering, discarding, or CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED close are the only options. |
| `7-59` | MUST | Packets containing CRYPTO frames that were discarded (post-handshake buffer policy) must still be acknowledged, since the packet itself was received and processed. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Packets containing discarded CRYPTO frames MUST be acknowledged because the packet has been received and processed by the transport even though the CRYPTO frame was discarded. | quic/ack_tracker.go + conn_recv.go: packet is registered as received (ack-eliciting) even when its CRYPTO payload is discarded. |

### 9000 section 8 - Address Validation

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `8-1` | MUST | An endpoint receiving packets from a not-yet-validated address must limit data sent to that address to three times the data received from it (anti-amplification limit). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | after receiving packets from an address that is not yet validated, an endpoint MUST limit the amount of data it sends to the unvalidated address to three times the amount | quic/conn.go — for a client the server address is validated on first successfully processed packet, so the limit never binds after that. The listener role is no longer exempt: since #715 it sends a CONNECTION_CLOSE in the Initial space to a peer whose address it has not validated, when TLS refuses the ClientHello. That is one ~42-byte datagram against an Initial of at least 1200, a ratio of about 0.035, so the limit does not bind there either — but no budget counter exists on that path, and the reasoning is carried in initialCloseDatagram's doc comment rather than enforced. |
| `8-2` | informative | Once an endpoint has successfully processed a Handshake packet from the peer, it can treat the peer address as validated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Once an endpoint has successfully processed a Handshake packet from the peer, it can consider the peer address to have been validated. | quic/conn_crypto.go / handshake.go — check the client marks the server address validated (if tracked at all) at Handshake-packet processing. |
| `8-3` | MAY | An endpoint may consider the peer address validated if the peer uses a connection ID the endpoint chose containing at least 64 bits of entropy. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | an endpoint MAY consider the peer address validated if the peer uses a connection ID chosen by the endpoint and the connection ID contains at least 64 bits of entropy | quic/connid.go — server-side validation shortcut; no-op for a client-only implementation. |
| `8-4` | informative | The client validates the server address as part of successfully processing any packet, because server Initial keys derive from the client's first Destination Connection ID. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the value of the Destination Connection ID field in its first Initial packet allows it to validate the server address as a part of successfully processing any packet | quic/initial.go + conn_recv.go — Initial keys derived from client-chosen DCID; successful decrypt of any server packet is the address validation, no extra mechanism needed. |
| `8-5` | MUST NOT | Prior to validating the client address, a server must not send more than three times the bytes it has received. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Prior to validating the client address, servers MUST NOT send more than three times as many bytes as the number of bytes they have received. | Server-only; quic/server.go test peer may skip, but note it in test-peer docs. |
| `8-6` | MUST | For anti-amplification accounting, servers must count all payload bytes received in datagrams uniquely attributed to a connection, including datagrams whose packets are all discarded. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers MUST count all of the payload bytes received in datagrams that are uniquely attributed to a single connection | Server-only accounting rule. It became reachable when quic/listener.go gained a server role, and #715 made that role send its first byte to an unvalidated peer — but the listener sends at most one small close there and never accumulates a budget, so there is nothing yet to account. Revisit if the listener grows a send path that can outrun what it received. |
| `8-7` | MUST | Clients must ensure every UDP datagram containing an Initial packet has a UDP payload of at least 1200 bytes, adding PADDING frames as necessary. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MUST ensure that UDP datagrams containing Initial packets have UDP payloads of at least 1200 bytes, adding PADDING frames as necessary. | quic/initial.go + packet_write.go — verify every Initial-carrying datagram (first flight, coalesced flights, and Retry re-sends) pads the datagram, not the packet, to >=1200. |
| `8-8` | MUST | To prevent the handshake anti-amplification deadlock, clients must send a packet on Probe Timeout (PTO). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | To prevent this deadlock, clients MUST send a packet on a Probe Timeout (PTO) | quic/pto.go — PTO must fire and transmit during handshake even when nothing is outstanding-unacked from client's perspective. |
| `8-9` | MUST | On PTO the client must send an Initial packet in a >=1200-byte UDP datagram if it lacks Handshake keys, and otherwise send a Handshake packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the client MUST send an Initial packet in a UDP datagram that contains at least 1200 bytes if it does not have Handshake keys, and otherwise send a Handshake packet | quic/pto.go — check probe-packet selection: Initial (padded to 1200) before Handshake keys exist, Handshake packet after. |
| `8-10` | informative | The address validation token in the Initial packet is delivered to the client either via a Retry packet or via a NEW_TOKEN frame on a previous connection. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | This token is delivered to the client during connection establishment with a Retry packet | quic/retry.go + frame.go — client must accept tokens from both sources: Retry token echo and NEW_TOKEN frame parsing. |
| `8-11` | informative | Client sending is constrained only by the congestion controller, not by pre-validation send limits. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients are only constrained by the congestion controller. | quic/cc.go — confirm no anti-amplification gate exists on the client send path. |
| `8-12` | MUST | A token sent in NEW_TOKEN or Retry must be constructed so the server can identify how it was provided to the client. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A token sent in a NEW_TOKEN frame or a Retry packet MUST be constructed in a way that allows the server to identify how it was provided to a client. | Server-only token construction; client treats tokens as opaque bytes. |
| `8-13` | MUST | A Retry-provided token must be repeated by the client in all Initial packets it sends for that connection after receiving the Retry packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | This token MUST be repeated by the client in all Initial packets it sends for that connection after it receives the Retry packet. | quic/retry.go + initial.go — after Retry, ALL subsequent Initials (including PTO retransmissions) must carry the token, not just the next one. |
| `8-14` | informative | A server that processes an Initial containing a Retry-provided token cannot send another Retry; it can only refuse or permit the connection. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | In response to processing an Initial packet containing a token that was provided in a Retry packet, a server cannot send another Retry packet | quic/retry.go — corollary for the client: a second Retry after the first must be discarded (enforced per §17.2.5); verify the client drops it. |
| `8-15` | SHOULD | On receiving a client Initial with an invalid Retry token that is otherwise valid, the server should immediately close the connection with an INVALID_TOKEN error. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | the server SHOULD immediately close (Section 10.2) the connection with an INVALID_TOKEN error. | quic/errors.go — client only needs INVALID_TOKEN (0x0b) defined so a server CONNECTION_CLOSE carrying it is parsed and surfaced correctly. |
| `8-16` | MAY | A server may provide clients with an address validation token during one connection for use on a subsequent connection (via NEW_TOKEN). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY provide clients with an address validation token during one connection that can be used on a subsequent connection. | quic/frame.go + conn_recv.go — client must at minimum parse NEW_TOKEN frames correctly even if it discards the token. |
| `8-17` | MUST | A client using a NEW_TOKEN-provided token must include it in all Initial packets it sends, unless a Retry replaces the token with a newer one. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client MUST include the token in all Initial packets it sends, unless a Retry replaces the token with a newer one. | quic/initial.go — if the client ever sends a stored token, it must go in every Initial; also a Retry token must override a NEW_TOKEN token for the rest of the attempt. |
| `8-18` | MUST NOT | A client must not use a token provided in a Retry packet for future connections. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client MUST NOT use the token provided in a Retry for future connections. | quic/retry.go — verify the Retry token is scoped to the current Conn/attempt and never persisted across Dial calls. |
| `8-19` | MAY | Servers may discard any Initial packet that does not carry the expected token. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers MAY discard any Initial packet that does not carry the expected token. | Server-only; irrelevant to quic/ client. |
| `8-20` | SHOULD | A token sent in NEW_TOKEN should have an expiration time, explicit or derivable from an issued timestamp. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | a token SHOULD have an expiration time, which could be either an explicit expiration time or an issued timestamp | Server-only token lifetime policy. |
| `8-21` | MUST NOT | A NEW_TOKEN-issued token must not include information linkable by an observer to the issuing connection (e.g., prior connection ID or address, unless encrypted). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A token issued with NEW_TOKEN MUST NOT include information that would allow values to be linked by an observer to the connection on which it was issued. | Server-only token construction. |
| `8-22` | MUST | A server must ensure every NEW_TOKEN frame it sends is unique across all clients, except retransmissions repairing losses of previously sent NEW_TOKEN frames. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST ensure that every NEW_TOKEN frame it sends is unique across all clients, with the exception of those sent to repair losses of previously sent NEW_TOKEN frames | Server-only. |
| `8-23` | MAY | Information letting the server distinguish Retry tokens from NEW_TOKEN tokens may be accessible to entities other than the server. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Information that allows the server to distinguish between tokens from Retry and NEW_TOKEN MAY be accessible to entities other than the server. | Server-only token format latitude. |
| `8-24` | SHOULD | When connecting to a server for which the client retains an applicable, unused token, the client should include that token in the Token field of its Initial packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | When connecting to a server for which the client retains an applicable and unused token, it SHOULD include that token in the Token field of its Initial packet. | quic/ + http3 dial path — client likely has no cross-connection token cache; document as an accepted SHOULD gap or implement a per-server token store. |
| `8-25` | MUST NOT | A client must not include a token that is not applicable to the server it is connecting to, unless it knows the issuing and target servers jointly manage tokens. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT include a token that is not applicable to the server that it is connecting to | If a token store is ever added, key it by server identity (certificate names); until then vacuously satisfied — note in RFC_COVERAGE. |
| `8-26` | MAY | A client may use a token from any previous connection to that server. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY use a token from any previous connection to that server. | Optional; only relevant if a token cache is implemented. |
| `8-27` | MUST | A token obtained in a Retry packet must be used immediately during that connection attempt and cannot be used in subsequent connection attempts. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | a token obtained in a Retry packet MUST be used immediately during the connection attempt and cannot be used in subsequent connection attempts. | quic/retry.go — Retry token applied to the in-progress attempt only; pairs with 8-18 (no persistence). |
| `8-28` | SHOULD NOT | A client should not reuse a NEW_TOKEN-frame token across different connection attempts, to avoid linkability by on-path entities. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client SHOULD NOT reuse a token from a NEW_TOKEN frame for different connection attempts. | If a token store is added: mark tokens used-once; currently vacuous. |
| `8-29` | informative | When holding multiple tokens, sending the most recent unused token is most likely to be effective for the server. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | sending the most recent unused token is most likely to be effective. | Token-store selection policy if implemented: newest unused first. |
| `8-30` | MUST | A server receiving an Initial with a token must attempt to validate it, unless address validation is already complete. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | When a server receives an Initial packet with an address validation token, it MUST attempt to validate the token, unless it has already completed address validation. | Server-only. |
| `8-31` | SHOULD | If the token is invalid, the server should proceed as if the client had no validated address, including potentially sending a Retry packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the token is invalid, then the server SHOULD proceed as if the client did not have a validated address, including potentially sending a Retry packet. | Server-only (applies to NEW_TOKEN-sourced tokens; contrast Retry tokens, 8-15). |
| `8-32` | SHOULD | If token validation succeeds, the server should allow the handshake to proceed. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the validation succeeds, the server SHOULD then allow the handshake to proceed. | Server-only. |
| `8-33` | MAY | Clients may use tokens obtained on one connection for any connection attempt using the same version, regardless of other connection properties. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MAY use tokens obtained on one connection for any connection attempt using the same version. | Token store (if added) must be QUIC-version-scoped; v1-only client makes this trivial. |
| `8-34` | MUST | An address validation token must be difficult to guess (e.g., contain at least 128 bits of entropy). <br>_(server [not-client])_ | An address validation token MUST be difficult to guess. | Server-only token generation. |
| `8-35` | MUST | The token must be covered by integrity protection against modification or falsification by clients. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | the token MUST be covered by integrity protection against modification or falsification by clients. | Server-only. |
| `8-36` | SHOULD | Tokens sent in Retry packets should include information letting the server verify that the client source IP address and port remain constant. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Tokens sent in Retry packets SHOULD include information that allows the server to verify that the source IP address and port in client packets remain constant. | Server-only. |
| `8-37` | MUST | Tokens sent in NEW_TOKEN frames must include information letting the server verify the client IP address has not changed since issuance. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Tokens sent in NEW_TOKEN frames MUST include information that allows the server to verify that the client IP address has not changed from when the token was issued | Server-only. |
| `8-38` | MUST | If the client IP address has changed from a NEW_TOKEN token's issuance, the server must adhere to the anti-amplification limit. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | If the client IP address has changed, the server MUST adhere to the anti-amplification limit | Server-only. |
| `8-39` | MUST | Servers must ensure that replay of tokens is prevented or limited. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers MUST ensure that replay of tokens is prevented or limited. | Server-only. |
| `8-40` | SHOULD | Servers should accept Retry-packet tokens only for a short time, since clients return them immediately. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers SHOULD ensure that tokens sent in Retry packets are only accepted for a short time, as they are returned immediately by clients. | Server-only. |
| `8-41` | SHOULD NOT | NEW_TOKEN-frame tokens need longer validity but should not be accepted multiple times. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Tokens that are provided in NEW_TOKEN frames (Section 19.7) need to be valid for longer but SHOULD NOT be accepted multiple times. | Server-only. |
| `8-42` | MAY | Tokens may include additional information about clients to further narrow applicability or reuse. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | tokens MAY include additional information about clients to further narrow applicability or reuse. | Server-only. |
| `8-43` | informative | Path validation can be initiated at any time by either endpoint; therefore even a non-migrating client can receive PATH_CHALLENGE from the server. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Path validation can be used at any time by either endpoint. | quic/conn_recv.go — justifies why PATH_CHALLENGE response machinery is mandatory in a no-migration client (pathchallenge_test.go exists; confirm coverage). |
| `8-44` | MAY | An endpoint may include other frames alongside PATH_CHALLENGE and PATH_RESPONSE frames (e.g., PADDING for PMTUD, or its own PATH_CHALLENGE with a PATH_RESPONSE). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MAY include other frames with the PATH_CHALLENGE and PATH_RESPONSE frames used for path validation. | quic/packet_write.go — PATH_RESPONSE may coalesce with other frames in one packet; no isolation requirement. |
| `8-45` | MAY | An endpoint may send multiple PATH_CHALLENGE frames (in separate packets) to guard against packet loss. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MAY send multiple PATH_CHALLENGE frames to guard against packet loss. | Initiator-side; no-migration client never initiates path validation. |
| `8-46` | SHOULD NOT | An endpoint should not send multiple PATH_CHALLENGE frames in a single packet. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | an endpoint SHOULD NOT send multiple PATH_CHALLENGE frames in a single packet. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-47` | SHOULD NOT | An endpoint should not probe a new path with PATH_CHALLENGE packets more frequently than it would send Initial packets. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint SHOULD NOT probe a new path with packets containing a PATH_CHALLENGE frame more frequently than it would send an Initial packet. | Initiator-side pacing; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-48` | MUST | The endpoint must use unpredictable data in every PATH_CHALLENGE frame so it can associate the peer's response with the corresponding challenge. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | The endpoint MUST use unpredictable data in every PATH_CHALLENGE frame so that it can associate the peer's response with the corresponding PATH_CHALLENGE. | Initiator-side; if quic/ ever sends PATH_CHALLENGE (frame_write.go supports it), payload must be crypto-random. |
| `8-49` | MUST | Datagrams containing a PATH_CHALLENGE frame must be expanded to at least 1200 bytes, unless the anti-amplification limit for the path prevents it. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MUST expand datagrams that contain a PATH_CHALLENGE frame to at least the smallest allowed maximum datagram size of 1200 bytes | Initiator-side; would live in quic/packet_write.go if client ever probes. |
| `8-50` | MUST | If the anti-amplification limit prevented expanding the PATH_CHALLENGE datagram, the endpoint must later perform a second path validation with a >=1200-byte PATH_CHALLENGE datagram to validate path MTU. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | the endpoint MUST perform a second path validation by sending a PATH_CHALLENGE frame in a datagram of at least 1200 bytes. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client (client has no anti-amp limit). |
| `8-51` | MUST NOT | Endpoints must not discard datagrams that appear too small when they contain PATH_CHALLENGE or PATH_RESPONSE frames. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | endpoints MUST NOT discard datagrams that appear to be too small when they contain PATH_CHALLENGE or PATH_RESPONSE. | quic/conn_recv.go — ensure no minimum-datagram-size drop is applied before frame parsing when the datagram carries PATH_CHALLENGE/PATH_RESPONSE. |
| `8-52` | MUST | On receiving a PATH_CHALLENGE frame, an endpoint must respond by echoing its data in a PATH_RESPONSE frame. <br>_(endpoint,receiver [CLIENT])_ | On receiving a PATH_CHALLENGE frame, an endpoint MUST respond by echoing the data contained in the PATH_CHALLENGE frame in a PATH_RESPONSE frame. | quic/conn_recv.go + conn.go — PATH_CHALLENGE handler must queue a PATH_RESPONSE with the exact 8-byte payload (pathresp_test.go exists; verify). |
| `8-53` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not delay transmission of a packet containing a PATH_RESPONSE frame unless constrained by congestion control. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT delay transmission of a packet containing a PATH_RESPONSE frame unless constrained by congestion control. | quic/conn.go — PATH_RESPONSE must be sent promptly, not batched behind ack-delay or write coalescing timers. |
| `8-54` | MUST | A PATH_RESPONSE frame must be sent on the network path where the corresponding PATH_CHALLENGE was received. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | A PATH_RESPONSE frame MUST be sent on the network path where the PATH_CHALLENGE frame was received. | quic/conn.go — trivially satisfied on a single-path client (one UDP socket); note as vacuous in RFC_COVERAGE. |
| `8-55` | MUST NOT | The endpoint that initiates path validation must not enforce the same-path requirement on received PATH_RESPONSE frames, as that would enable an attack on migration. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | This requirement MUST NOT be enforced by the endpoint that initiates path validation, as that would enable an attack on migration | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-56` | MUST | Datagrams containing a PATH_RESPONSE frame must be expanded to at least 1200 bytes. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST expand datagrams that contain a PATH_RESPONSE frame to at least the smallest allowed maximum datagram size of 1200 bytes. | quic/packet_write.go — when a packet carries PATH_RESPONSE, pad the enclosing datagram to >=1200 (same mechanism as Initial padding); verify this exists. |
| `8-57` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not expand a PATH_RESPONSE-carrying datagram if the resulting data would exceed the anti-amplification limit. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | an endpoint MUST NOT expand the datagram containing the PATH_RESPONSE if the resulting data exceeds the anti-amplification limit. | quic/packet_write.go — client has no anti-amp limit toward a validated server, so unconditional expansion is fine; document why the exception is unreachable. |
| `8-58` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not send more than one PATH_RESPONSE frame in response to one PATH_CHALLENGE frame; the peer sends more challenges to evoke more responses. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT send more than one PATH_RESPONSE frame in response to one PATH_CHALLENGE frame | quic/loss.go + conn.go — PATH_RESPONSE must NOT be retransmitted on loss (excluded from the retransmit-on-loss frame set); each received PATH_CHALLENGE yields exactly one response. |
| `8-59` | informative | Path validation succeeds when a PATH_RESPONSE is received containing the data sent in a previous PATH_CHALLENGE. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | Path validation succeeds when a PATH_RESPONSE frame is received that contains the data that was sent in a previous PATH_CHALLENGE frame. | Initiator-side success criterion; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-60` | informative | A PATH_RESPONSE received on any network path validates the path on which the corresponding PATH_CHALLENGE was sent. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | A PATH_RESPONSE frame received on any network path validates the path on which the PATH_CHALLENGE was sent. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-61` | MUST | If a PATH_CHALLENGE was sent in a non-expanded datagram and the response validates the address, the endpoint must initiate another path validation with an expanded datagram to verify path MTU. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | the endpoint MUST initiate another path validation with an expanded datagram to verify that the path supports the required MTU. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-62` | informative | Receipt of an acknowledgment for a packet containing a PATH_CHALLENGE is not adequate path validation, since acknowledgments can be spoofed. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | Receipt of an acknowledgment for a packet containing a PATH_CHALLENGE frame is not adequate validation, since the acknowledgment can be spoofed by a malicious peer. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-63` | SHOULD | Endpoints should abandon path validation based on a timer; path validation only fails when the validating endpoint abandons it. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | Endpoints SHOULD abandon path validation based on a timer. | Initiator-side; N/A for no-migration client. |
| `8-64` | RECOMMENDED | The recommended path validation abandonment timer is three times the larger of the current PTO or the PTO for the new path computed with kInitialRtt. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | A value of three times the larger of the current PTO or the PTO for the new path (using kInitialRtt, as defined in [QUIC-RECOVERY]) is RECOMMENDED. | Initiator-side timer; N/A for no-migration client (constant would live in quic/pto.go). |
| `8-65` | MAY | An endpoint with no valid network path to its peer may signal this with the NO_VIABLE_PATH connection error, which is only possible when a path exists but does not support the required MTU. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that has no valid network path to its peer MAY signal this using the NO_VIABLE_PATH connection error | quic/errors.go — NO_VIABLE_PATH (0x10) must be defined so a peer CONNECTION_CLOSE carrying it parses/reports correctly; client never needs to emit it. |

### 9000 section 9 - Connection Migration (RFC 9000 §9–9.7)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `9-1` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not initiate connection migration before the TLS handshake is confirmed. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT initiate connection migration before the handshake is confirmed, as defined in Section 4.1.2 of [QUIC-TLS]. | quic/conn.go — client never rebinds its UDP socket mid-connection; verify one socket is bound at Dial and never re-bound, so this holds by construction. |
| `9-2` | MUST NOT | If the peer sent disable_active_migration, the endpoint must not send any packets (including probing packets) from a different local address than used in the handshake, unless acting on preferred_address. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | an endpoint also MUST NOT send packets (including probing packets; see Section 9.1) from a different local address to the address the peer used during the handshake | quic/transport_params.go + conn.go — check the client honors a server-sent disable_active_migration; satisfied by construction if all sends go through the single Dial-time socket (verify GSO path in gro/gso uses the same socket). |
| `9-3` | MUST | If the peer violates disable_active_migration, the endpoint must either drop those packets without generating a Stateless Reset, or proceed with path validation and allow migration. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | the endpoint MUST either drop the incoming packets on that path without generating a Stateless Reset or proceed with path validation and allow the peer to migrate | quic/transport_params.go — check whether client sends disable_active_migration; if so, conn_recv.go must silently drop new-path packets (satisfied: no stateless-reset generation is a stated non-goal). |
| `9-4` | MUST | An endpoint must perform path validation if it detects any change to a peer's address (e.g., NAT rebinding), unless that address was previously validated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST perform path validation (Section 8.2) if it detects any change to a peer's address, unless it has previously validated that address. | quic/conn_recv.go — for a client the peer is the server; a changed server source address is instead discarded per 9-9/9-45. Verify conn_recv.go compares datagram source addr to the dialed server addr. |
| `9-5` | MAY | When an endpoint has no validated path on which to send packets, it may discard all connection state. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | When an endpoint has no validated path on which to send packets, it MAY discard connection state. | quic/close.go — client path failure surfaces as idle/PTO timeout leading to close; no separate action needed, note as covered. |
| `9-6` | MAY | An endpoint capable of connection migration may wait for a new path to become available before discarding connection state. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint capable of connection migration MAY wait for a new path to become available before discarding connection state. | N/A — client is not migration-capable by design; quic/close.go needs no wait-for-path logic. |
| `9-7` | informative | QUIC v1 limits migration to new client addresses (except server preferred address); clients initiate all migrations. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This document limits migration of connections to new client addresses, except as described in Section 9.6. | quic/conn.go doc comment — confirms the client-only no-migration design is spec-shaped: the server can never legitimately migrate toward us mid-connection. |
| `9-8` | informative | Servers do not send non-probing packets toward a client address until they have seen a non-probing packet from that address. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers do not send non-probing packets (see Section 9.1) toward a client address until they see a non-probing packet from that address. | Server-role only; not applicable to quic/ client (test peer is net-stack based). |
| `9-9` | MUST | A client must discard packets that arrive from an unknown server address. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If a client receives packets from an unknown server address, the client MUST discard these packets. | quic/conn_recv.go — key check: verify every received datagram's source address is compared against the dialed server address and dropped on mismatch BEFORE any parsing/decryption side effects. |
| `9-10` | MAY | An endpoint may probe reachability from a new local address using path validation before migrating to it. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MAY probe for peer reachability from a new local address using path validation (Section 8.2) prior to migrating the connection to the new local address. | N/A — no migration/probing in quic/; no PATH_CHALLENGE initiation needed (frame_write.go need not emit PATH_CHALLENGE). |
| `9-11` | informative | Failure of path validation only makes that path unusable; it does not end the connection unless no valid alternative path exists. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | Failure to validate a path does not cause the connection to end unless there are no valid alternative paths available. | N/A without migration; quic/close.go unaffected. |
| `9-12` | informative | PATH_CHALLENGE, PATH_RESPONSE, NEW_CONNECTION_ID, and PADDING are 'probing frames'; all other frame types are 'non-probing frames'. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | PATH_CHALLENGE, PATH_RESPONSE, NEW_CONNECTION_ID, and PADDING frames are "probing frames", and all other frames are "non-probing frames". | quic/frame.go — probing/non-probing classification only matters for migration path selection; not needed in a non-migrating client, but document the taxonomy if a classifier is ever added. |
| `9-13` | informative | A packet containing only probing frames is a 'probing packet'; a packet containing any other frame is a 'non-probing packet'. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | A packet containing only probing frames is a "probing packet", and a packet containing any other frame is a "non-probing packet". | quic/packet.go — same as 9-12; per-packet classification unnecessary without migration support. |
| `9-14` | informative | An endpoint migrates a connection to a new local address by sending packets containing non-probing frames from that address. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint can migrate a connection to a new local address by sending packets containing non-probing frames from that address. | N/A — quic/ client never sends from a second local address. |
| `9-15` | MAY | A migrating endpoint may defer path validation of the new path until after the peer sends its next non-probing frame to the new address. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MAY defer path validation until after a peer sends the next non-probing frame to its new address. | N/A — migration not implemented in quic/. |
| `9-16` | informative | On migration the endpoint validates ECN capability of the new path per Section 13.4 (and resets CC/RTT per Section 9.4). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | the endpoint validates ECN capability as described in Section 13.4. | N/A — no migration; ECN validation per §13.4 is a separate item (check whether quic/cc.go implements ECN at all). |
| `9-17` | MUST | A recipient that permits a peer's migration must send subsequent packets to the new peer address. <br>_(receiver,server [not-client])_ | If the recipient permits the migration, it MUST send subsequent packets to the new peer address | Server-role (only clients migrate in v1); quic/ client discards new server addresses instead (9-9). |
| `9-18` | MUST | A recipient permitting migration must initiate path validation of the new peer address if not already underway. <br>_(receiver,server [not-client])_ | MUST initiate path validation (Section 8.2) to verify the peer's ownership of the address if validation is not already underway | Server-role only; no action in quic/. |
| `9-19` | informative | An endpoint changes its sending address only in response to the highest-numbered non-probing packet, so reordered packets do not revert the path. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint only changes the address to which it sends packets in response to the highest-numbered non-probing packet. | Server-role migration response; N/A for quic/ client. |
| `9-20` | MAY | An endpoint may send data to an unvalidated peer address. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MAY send data to an unvalidated peer address | Server-role migration response; client only ever sends to the handshake-validated server address. |
| `9-21` | MUST | An endpoint sending to an unvalidated peer address must protect against address-spoofing attacks per Sections 9.3.1 and 9.3.2 (anti-amplification limits). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | it MUST protect against potential attacks as described in Sections 9.3.1 and 9.3.2 | Server-role; the client never sends to an unvalidated address in quic/. |
| `9-22` | MAY | An endpoint may skip path validation of a peer address if that address has been seen recently (e.g., returning to a previously validated path). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | An endpoint MAY skip validation of a peer address if that address has been seen recently. | N/A — no migration state kept in quic/. |
| `9-23` | informative | After switching its non-probing traffic to a new address, an endpoint can abandon path validation attempts for other addresses. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | After changing the address to which it sends non-probing packets, an endpoint can abandon any path validation for other addresses. | N/A — no concurrent path validations in quic/. |
| `9-24` | SHOULD | After verifying a new client address, the server should send the client fresh address validation tokens. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | After verifying a new client address, the server SHOULD send new address validation tokens (Section 8) to the client. | Server duty; client side only needs NEW_TOKEN receipt handling (see quic/retry.go token handling, covered under §8/§19.7). |
| `9-25` | informative | Until a peer's new address is deemed valid, an endpoint limits the amount of data sent to that address (anti-amplification, Section 8). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | Until a peer's address is deemed valid, an endpoint limits the amount of data it sends to that address; see Section 8. | Server-role during migration; client's server address is validated by the handshake itself. |
| `9-26` | informative | An endpoint that skips validation of a recently seen peer address does not need to limit its sending rate to it. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | If an endpoint skips validation of a peer address as described above, it does not need to limit its sending rate. | N/A — no migration in quic/. |
| `9-27` | MUST | When validation of a new peer address fails, the endpoint must revert to using the last validated peer address. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | an endpoint MUST revert to using the last validated peer address when validation of a new peer address fails | Server-role (spurious-migration defense); N/A for quic/ client which never re-targets sends. |
| `9-28` | MUST | If validation of a new peer address fails and the endpoint has no state for the last validated address, it must close the connection silently by discarding all connection state. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | If an endpoint has no state about the last validated peer address, it MUST close the connection silently by discarding all connection state. | Server-role; quic/close.go has no such branch and needs none. |
| `9-29` | MAY | After silently discarding state, an endpoint may send a Stateless Reset in response to further incoming packets. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | an endpoint MAY send a Stateless Reset in response to any further incoming packets | Stateless reset generation is an explicit non-goal of quic/; MAY-level, so omitting is conformant. |
| `9-30` | MUST | In response to an apparent peer migration, an endpoint must validate the previously active path using a PATH_CHALLENGE frame (off-path forwarding defense). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | In response to an apparent migration, endpoints MUST validate the previously active path using a PATH_CHALLENGE frame. | Server-role migration response; quic/ client never observes a legitimate peer migration. |
| `9-31` | SHOULD | An endpoint receiving a PATH_CHALLENGE on its active path should send a non-probing packet in response (in addition to the mandatory PATH_RESPONSE of §8.2). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that receives a PATH_CHALLENGE on an active path SHOULD send a non-probing packet in response. | quic/conn_recv.go + frame_write.go — client CAN receive PATH_CHALLENGE (server validating after client NAT rebinding); verify PATH_CHALLENGE is parsed and answered with PATH_RESPONSE, and check whether an ack-eliciting/non-probing packet accompanies it. |
| `9-32` | MUST NOT | Packets sent on the old path must not contribute to congestion control or RTT estimation for the new path. <br>_(endpoint,sender [not-client])_ | Packets sent on the old path MUST NOT contribute to congestion control or RTT estimation for the new path. | quic/cc.go + rtt.go — single-path client; no per-path separation needed. Flag if migration is ever added. |
| `9-33` | MUST | On confirming the peer's ownership of its new address, an endpoint must immediately reset the congestion controller and RTT estimator to initial values, unless only the port changed. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | On confirming a peer's ownership of its new address, an endpoint MUST immediately reset the congestion controller and round-trip time estimator for the new path to initial values | quic/cc.go, bbr.go, rtt.go — no reset-on-migration hook exists or is needed; becomes mandatory if migration is added. |
| `9-34` | MAY | For port-only changes in the peer address (typical NAT rebinding), the endpoint may retain congestion control state and RTT estimate instead of resetting. <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | the endpoint MAY instead retain its congestion control state and round-trip estimate in those cases instead of reverting to initial values | N/A — same as 9-33; quic/cc.go untouched. |
| `9-35` | informative | A receiver of packets on multiple paths still sends ACK frames covering all received packets in one packet number space. <br>_(receiver [not-client])_ | A receiver of packets on multiple paths will still send ACK frames covering all received packets. | quic/ack_tracker.go — single path, single ACK space per PN space; already the only behavior possible. |
| `9-36` | SHOULD | A separate PATH_CHALLENGE retransmission timer should be set as described in Section 6.2.1 of QUIC-RECOVERY (PTO computation). <br>_(sender,endpoint [not-client])_ | This timer SHOULD be set as described in Section 6.2.1 of [QUIC-RECOVERY] and MUST NOT be more aggressive. | quic/pto.go — no PATH_CHALLENGE initiation in client, so no probe timer needed; binds any future migration work. |
| `9-37` | MUST NOT | The PATH_CHALLENGE retransmission timer must not be more aggressive than the PTO-based timer of QUIC-RECOVERY Section 6.2.1. <br>_(sender,endpoint [not-client])_ | This timer SHOULD be set as described in Section 6.2.1 of [QUIC-RECOVERY] and MUST NOT be more aggressive. | quic/pto.go — same as 9-36; N/A until PATH_CHALLENGE sending exists. |
| `9-38` | MAY | At any time an endpoint may switch the Destination Connection ID it sends with to a value not used on another path. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | At any time, endpoints MAY change the Destination Connection ID they transmit with to a value that has not been used on another path. | quic/connid.go — check whether NEW_CONNECTION_ID-supplied CIDs can be adopted as active DCID (rotation); MAY-level, so a fixed DCID is conformant but note the capability. |
| `9-39` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not reuse the same connection ID when sending from more than one local address. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST NOT reuse a connection ID when sending from more than one local address | quic/connid.go + packet_write.go — trivially satisfied by the single Dial-time socket; verify no code path (incl. gro/gso batching) sends via a second socket. |
| `9-40` | MUST NOT | An endpoint must not reuse a connection ID when sending to more than one destination address. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Similarly, an endpoint MUST NOT reuse a connection ID when sending to more than one destination address. | quic/packet_write.go — trivially satisfied: client sends only to the dialed server address; verify no alternate-destination send path exists. |
| `9-41` | MAY | If packets arrive from a new peer source address carrying the same DCID, the endpoint may continue using the current connection ID with the new remote address (same local address). <br>_(endpoint [not-client])_ | it MAY continue to use the current connection ID with the new remote address while still sending from the same local address | Server-role NAT-rebinding response; quic/ client discards new server source addresses (9-9). |
| `9-42` | SHOULD NOT | An endpoint should not initiate migration with a peer that requested a zero-length connection ID, since new-path traffic would be trivially linkable. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint SHOULD NOT initiate migration with a peer that has requested a zero-length connection ID | quic/conn.go — trivially satisfied (no migration ever initiated); no check needed. |
| `9-43` | SHOULD | Because changing address can reset the peer's congestion state, addresses should only be changed infrequently. <br>_(client,endpoint [not-client])_ | addresses SHOULD only be changed infrequently | Linkability advice for migrating clients; N/A — quic/ never changes address. |
| `9-44` | SHOULD | Endpoints should provide new connection IDs to the peer before the peer migrates, so migration is possible and paths cannot be correlated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | endpoints SHOULD provide new connection IDs before peers migrate | quic/connid.go — check whether the client issues NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames for its own CIDs; acceptable gap since the server never migrates in v1, but document the deviation. |
| `9-45` | SHOULD | A client that receives packets from a new server address it did not initiate migration to should discard those packets. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | If a client receives packets from a new server address when the client has not initiated a migration to that address, the client SHOULD discard these packets. | quic/conn_recv.go — same source-address gate as 9-9; since the client never migrates to a preferred address, EVERY non-dialed server source address must be dropped. |
