| `14-16-56` | informative | The Version field, packet numbers in packet headers, and the connection ID length fields in long headers are integers that do NOT use the varint encoding. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | packet numbers sent in the header (Section 17.1), and the length of connection IDs in long header packets (Section 17.2) are described using integers but do not use this encoding | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: Version = fixed 32-bit, packet number = 1–4 bytes per header PN-length bits, long-header CID lengths = single fixed bytes — none via bytesx varint. |

### 9000 section 17.1-17.2.2 - Packet Formats: Packet Number Encoding/Decoding, Long Header Packets, Version Negotiation Packet, Initial Packet, Abandoning Initial Packets

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `17.1-17.2.2-1` | informative | All numeric values in QUIC packet formats are encoded in network byte order (big endian) and all field sizes are given in bits. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | All numeric values are encoded in network byte order (that is, big endian), and all field sizes are in bits. | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go + internal/bytesx: verify all multi-byte fields read/written big-endian. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-2` | informative | Packet numbers are integers 0 to 2^62-1 and, when present in long or short headers, are encoded in 1 to 4 bytes using only the least significant bits. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | When present in long or short packet headers, they are encoded in 1 to 4 bytes. | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: PN encode supports 1-4 byte truncated encodings; full PN space is 62-bit. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-3` | informative | The encoded packet number field is protected with header protection per QUIC-TLS Section 5.4. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The encoded packet number is protected as described in Section 5.4 of [QUIC-TLS]. | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: header protection applied to PN bytes on send, removed before PN decode on receive. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-4` | MUST | Before any acknowledgment has been received for a packet number space, the sender must encode the full (untruncated) packet number. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | Prior to receiving an acknowledgment for a packet number space, the full packet number MUST be included; it is not to be truncated | quic/packet_write.go: PN length selection must use full encoding until first ACK arrives in that space (largest_acked absent case in Appendix A.2). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-5` | MUST | After an acknowledgment is received for a space, the sender must choose a packet number size representing more than twice the range between the largest acknowledged packet number and the packet number being sent. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | the sender MUST use a packet number size able to represent more than twice as large a range as the difference between the largest acknowledged packet number | quic/packet_write.go: PN encode picks num_bytes covering 2*(pn - largest_acked)+1 candidate values (Appendix A.2 algorithm). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-6` | SHOULD | An endpoint should choose a packet number encoding large enough that the packet number can still be recovered if the packet arrives after later-sent packets. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint SHOULD use a large enough packet number encoding to allow the packet number to be recovered even if the packet arrives after packets that are sent afterwards. | quic/packet_write.go: check PN size selection is not minimal-only; slack for reordering per A.2. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-7` | informative | The packet number encoding size is at least one bit more than the base-2 log of the count of contiguous unacknowledged packet numbers including the new packet. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | the size of the packet number encoding is at least one bit more than the base-2 logarithm of the number of contiguous unacknowledged packet numbers, including the new packet | quic/packet_write.go: encodePacketNumber bit-width formula matches this bound. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-8` | informative | A receiver removes header protection on the packet number before recovering the full packet number; recovering the full packet number is necessary to complete removal of packet protection (it feeds the AEAD nonce). <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | At a receiver, protection of the packet number is removed prior to recovering the full packet number. | quic/packet.go: receive path order — remove header protection, reconstruct full PN, then AEAD-decrypt payload with that PN. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-9` | informative | The full packet number is reconstructed from the number of significant bits present, their value, and the largest packet number received in a successfully authenticated packet. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | reconstructed based on the number of significant bits present, the value of those bits, and the largest packet number received in a successfully authenticated packet | quic/packet.go: decodePacketNumber uses largest authenticated PN per space as reference; unauthenticated packets must not update it. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-10` | informative | Packet number decoding selects the candidate value closest to the next expected packet, where next expected is the highest received packet number plus one (Appendix A.3 algorithm). <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | the packet number is decoded by finding the packet number value that is closest to the next expected packet | quic/packet.go: decodePacketNumber implements expected = largest+1, window centering with +/- pn_win/2 adjustment and 2^62-1 clamp per A.3. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-11` | informative | Long header packet layout: Header Form (1)=1, Fixed Bit (1)=1, Long Packet Type (2), Type-Specific Bits (4), Version (32), DCID Length (8), DCID (0..160), SCID Length (8), SCID (0..160), Type-Specific Payload. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Header Form (1) = 1, Fixed Bit (1) = 1, Long Packet Type (2), Type-Specific Bits (4), Version (32), Destination Connection ID Length (8) | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: long header field order and bit layout match Figure 13 exactly. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-12` | informative | Long headers are used for packets sent before 1-RTT keys are established; once 1-RTT keys are available a sender switches to the short header. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | Long headers are used for packets that are sent prior to the establishment of 1-RTT keys. | quic/send.go + packet_write.go: after 1-RTT keys installed, application packets use short header only. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-13` | informative | Header Form: the most significant bit (0x80) of byte 0 is set to 1 for long headers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The most significant bit (0x80) of byte 0 (the first byte) is set to 1 for long headers. | quic/packet.go: header form discrimination on b0&0x80; packet_write.go sets it on long headers. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-14` | informative | Fixed Bit: bit 0x40 of byte 0 is set to 1 in all long header packets except Version Negotiation packets. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | The next bit (0x40) of byte 0 is set to 1, unless the packet is a Version Negotiation packet. | quic/packet_write.go: every emitted long header sets 0x40; packet.go: do not require fixed bit on Version Negotiation packets. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-15` | MUST | A received packet with a zero Fixed Bit is not a valid QUIC v1 packet and must be discarded. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | Packets containing a zero value for this bit are not valid packets in this version and MUST be discarded. | quic/packet.go: receive path drops packets with b0&0x40==0 (except Version Negotiation, identified by Version==0 first). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-16` | informative | Long Packet Type: bits with mask 0x30 of byte 0 carry the long packet type, per Table 5. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The next two bits (those with a mask of 0x30) of byte 0 contain a packet type. | quic/packet.go: long type extracted as (b0>>4)&0x03 (mask 0x30). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-17` | informative | Type-Specific Bits: the semantics of the low four bits (mask 0x0f) of byte 0 depend on the packet type. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The semantics of the lower four bits (those with a mask of 0x0f) of byte 0 are determined by the packet type. | quic/packet.go: low nibble interpreted per type (Reserved+PN-length for Initial/0-RTT/Handshake; unused for Retry). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-18` | informative | Version: a 32-bit field immediately after byte 0 indicating the QUIC version in use, determining interpretation of remaining fields. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The QUIC Version is a 32-bit field that follows the first byte. | quic/packet.go: version read as big-endian uint32 at offset 1; interpretation branches on it before parsing type-specific fields. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-19` | MUST NOT | Destination Connection ID Length is an 8-bit unsigned integer and in QUIC version 1 must not exceed 20 bytes when sending. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | In QUIC version 1, this value MUST NOT exceed 20 bytes. | quic/packet_write.go + connid.go: never emit a long header with DCID length > 20. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-20` | MUST | An endpoint receiving a version 1 long header whose Destination Connection ID Length exceeds 20 must drop the packet. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints that receive a version 1 long header with a value larger than 20 MUST drop the packet. | quic/packet.go: long header parser rejects/drops when DCID len byte > 20. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-21` | MUST NOT | Source Connection ID Length is an 8-bit unsigned integer and in QUIC version 1 must not exceed 20 bytes when sending. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | In QUIC version 1, this value MUST NOT exceed 20 bytes. | quic/packet_write.go + connid.go: never emit a long header with SCID length > 20. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-22` | MUST | An endpoint receiving a version 1 long header whose Source Connection ID Length exceeds 20 must drop the packet. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints that receive a version 1 long header with a value larger than 20 MUST drop the packet. | quic/packet.go: long header parser rejects/drops when SCID len byte > 20. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-23` | SHOULD | To properly form Version Negotiation packets, servers should be able to read connection IDs longer than 20 bytes from other QUIC versions. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers SHOULD be able to read longer connection IDs from other QUIC versions. | N/A for client-only stack; note in quic/packet.go that this is server-side only. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-24` | informative | Long header packet type codepoints: 0x00 Initial, 0x01 0-RTT, 0x02 Handshake, 0x03 Retry (Table 5). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | In this version of QUIC, the following packet types with the long header are defined | quic/packet.go: long type constants map 0=Initial, 1=0-RTT, 2=Handshake, 3=Retry. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-25` | informative | The header form bit, DCID/SCID length and connection ID fields, and Version field of a long header are version independent; other first-byte fields are version specific. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The header form bit, Destination and Source Connection ID lengths, Destination and Source Connection ID fields, and Version fields of a long header packet are version independent. | quic/packet.go: version-independent fields parsed before branching on Version (needed to recognize Version Negotiation). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-26` | MUST | Reserved Bits (mask 0x0c of byte 0, header-protected) must be set to 0 prior to applying header protection when sending. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | The value included prior to protection MUST be set to 0. | quic/packet_write.go: byte 0 of Initial/0-RTT/Handshake packets has bits 0x0c clear before header protection. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-27` | MUST | An endpoint must treat non-zero Reserved Bits, observed after removing both packet and header protection, as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST treat receipt of a packet that has a non-zero value for these bits after removing both packet and header protection as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION | quic/packet.go + errors.go: reserved-bit check runs only after successful AEAD decryption and raises PROTOCOL_VIOLATION conn error. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-28` | informative | Discarding a packet for non-zero reserved bits after removing only header protection (before packet protection) can expose the endpoint to attacks, so the check must follow full decryption. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Discarding such a packet after only removing header protection can expose the endpoint to attacks | quic/packet.go: verify reserved-bit validation is sequenced after AEAD verify, never before. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-29` | informative | Packet Number Length: the two least significant bits (mask 0x03) of byte 0, header-protected, encode the Packet Number field length in bytes minus one. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | encoded as an unsigned two-bit integer that is one less than the length of the Packet Number field in bytes | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: pn_len = (b0&0x03)+1 on read; write stores len-1; bits covered by header protection mask. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-30` | informative | The long header Length field is a variable-length integer giving the byte length of the remainder of the packet, i.e. Packet Number plus Payload. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This is the length of the remainder of the packet (that is, the Packet Number and Payload fields) in bytes, encoded as a variable-length integer | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go + internal/bytesx: Length varint counts PN bytes plus payload bytes, enabling datagram coalescing boundaries. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-31` | informative | The Packet Number field is 1 to 4 bytes long, protected by header protection, with its length encoded in the Packet Number Length bits of byte 0. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This field is 1 to 4 bytes long. | quic/packet.go: PN field read of 1-4 bytes driven by unprotected PN-length bits. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-32` | informative | The Packet Payload contains a sequence of frames and is protected with packet protection (AEAD). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | This is the payload of the packet -- containing a sequence of frames -- that is protected using packet protection. | quic/packet.go + frame.go: payload decrypt then frame-sequence parse; no unencrypted frames in protected packets. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-33` | informative | A client identifies a received packet as a Version Negotiation packet by the Version field having the value 0. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | it will be identified as a Version Negotiation packet based on the Version field having a value of 0 | quic/packet.go: long-header dispatch checks Version==0 for VN before any v1 type/fixed-bit validation. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-34` | informative | A Version Negotiation packet is only sent by servers, as a response to a client packet carrying a version the server does not support; a client never sends one. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The Version Negotiation packet is a response to a client packet that contains a version that is not supported by the server. | quic/packet_write.go: client must have no VN emission path; receipt handled in packet.go/retry.go path. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-35` | informative | Version Negotiation packet layout: Header Form (1)=1, Unused (7), Version (32)=0, DCID Length (8), DCID (0..2040), SCID Length (8), SCID (0..2040), then a list of Supported Version (32) values. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Header Form (1) = 1, Unused (7), Version (32) = 0, Destination Connection ID Length (8), Destination Connection ID (0..2040) | quic/packet.go: VN parser accepts connection IDs up to 255 bytes (0..2040 bits), not capped at 20 like v1 headers. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-36` | MUST | Clients must ignore the value of the 7-bit Unused field in a Version Negotiation packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MUST ignore the value of this field. | quic/packet.go: VN parse ignores byte0 low 7 bits entirely (including fixed-bit position). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-37` | SHOULD | Servers should set bit 0x40 of the Unused field to 1 so Version Negotiation packets appear to have the Fixed Bit, for multiplexing coexistence. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers SHOULD set the most significant bit of this field (0x40) to 1 so that Version Negotiation packets appear to have the Fixed Bit field | Server-only emission rule; client side in quic/packet.go must not require the bit (covered by MUST-ignore). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-38` | MUST | The Version field of a Version Negotiation packet must be exactly 0x00000000. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The Version field of a Version Negotiation packet MUST be set to 0x00000000. | quic/packet.go: client treats only Version==0x00000000 as VN; any other value parsed as versioned packet. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-39` | MUST | The server must copy the Source Connection ID of the packet it received into the Destination Connection ID field of the Version Negotiation packet, so a client can validate the echo. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The server MUST include the value from the Source Connection ID field of the packet it receives in the Destination Connection ID field. | quic/packet.go (VN handling): client should verify VN DCID equals the SCID it sent; mismatched VN packets are off-path forgeries to discard. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-40` | MUST | The Source Connection ID of a Version Negotiation packet must be copied from the Destination Connection ID of the received packet (the client's initially random choice). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The value for Source Connection ID MUST be copied from the Destination Connection ID of the received packet | quic/packet.go (VN handling): client should verify VN SCID equals the DCID it originally sent before acting on the packet. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-41` | MUST NOT | Version-specific connection ID rules must not influence a server's decision about whether to send a Version Negotiation packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Version-specific rules for the connection ID therefore MUST NOT influence a decision about whether to send a Version Negotiation packet. | Server-only; no client code path in quic/. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-42` | informative | The remainder of a Version Negotiation packet after the connection IDs is a list of 32-bit versions the server supports. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The remainder of the Version Negotiation packet is a list of 32-bit versions that the server supports. | quic/packet.go: VN parser iterates trailing bytes as big-endian uint32 version list; length not divisible by 4 is malformed. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-43` | informative | A Version Negotiation packet is not acknowledged; it is only sent in response to a packet indicating an unsupported version. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A Version Negotiation packet is not acknowledged. | quic/ack_tracker.go + packet.go: VN packets must not enter ACK tracking or packet-number accounting (they carry no PN). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-44` | informative | A Version Negotiation packet has no Packet Number and no Length field, unlike other long header packets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The Version Negotiation packet does not include the Packet Number and Length fields present in other packets that use the long header form. | quic/packet.go: VN parse path skips PN/Length handling and header protection removal. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-45` | informative | Because it lacks Length and Packet Number fields, a Version Negotiation packet consumes an entire UDP datagram and cannot be coalesced with other packets. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Consequently, a Version Negotiation packet consumes an entire UDP datagram. | quic/packet.go: datagram coalescing loop must not attempt to parse further packets after a VN packet. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-46` | MUST NOT | A server must not send more than one Version Negotiation packet in response to a single UDP datagram. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST NOT send more than one Version Negotiation packet in response to a single UDP datagram. | Server-only emission rule; not applicable to client-only quic/. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-47` | informative | An Initial packet is a long header packet with type value 0x00; it carries the first CRYPTO frames for key exchange and ACK frames in either direction. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | An Initial packet uses long headers with a type value of 0x00. | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: Initial type codepoint 0 in bits 0x30; initial.go builds first flight. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-48` | informative | The Initial packet adds two fields between the Source Connection ID and Length fields: Token Length (varint) and Token; then Length (i), Packet Number (8..32), Packet Payload (8..). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Between the Source Connection ID and Length fields, there are two additional fields specific to the Initial packet. | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: Initial header field order SCID -> Token Length -> Token -> Length -> PN -> payload per Figure 15. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-49` | informative | Token Length is a variable-length integer giving the Token field length in bytes, and is 0 when no token is present. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | A variable-length integer specifying the length of the Token field, in bytes. This value is 0 if no token is present. | quic/packet_write.go + retry.go: client writes Token Length 0 normally, or the Retry/NEW_TOKEN token length when echoing one. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-50` | MUST | Initial packets sent by a server must set the Token Length field to 0. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Initial packets sent by the server MUST set the Token Length field to 0 | quic/packet.go: client-side parse of server Initial should enforce Token Length == 0 (pairs with fact -51). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-51` | MUST | A client receiving an Initial packet with a non-zero Token Length must either discard the packet or generate a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | clients that receive an Initial packet with a non-zero Token Length field MUST either discard the packet or generate a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION | quic/packet.go + errors.go: server Initial with Token Length != 0 -> drop or PROTOCOL_VIOLATION; verify one of the two happens. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-52` | informative | The Token field carries the value previously provided to the client in a Retry packet or NEW_TOKEN frame. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The value of the token that was previously provided in a Retry packet or NEW_TOKEN frame | quic/retry.go + packet_write.go: after Retry, subsequent client Initials carry the Retry token verbatim. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-53` | informative | Initial packets are protected with connection- and version-specific Initial keys, which prevent spoofing by off-path attackers but give no confidentiality against observers. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Initial packets are protected with connection- and version-specific keys (Initial keys) as described in [QUIC-TLS] | quic/initial.go: Initial secrets derived from client's original DCID and v1 salt; keys are per-connection and per-version. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-54` | informative | The Initial packet type is used for any packet containing an initial cryptographic handshake message, including new packets created after receiving a Retry packet. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The client and server use the Initial packet type for any packet that contains an initial cryptographic handshake message. | quic/retry.go + initial.go: post-Retry ClientHello retransmission still goes out in Initial packets (with new keys from the Retry SCID). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-55` | MAY | A server may send multiple Initial packets, since the key exchange can require multiple round trips or retransmissions. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY send multiple Initial packets. | quic/packet.go + initial.go: client must accept and process a sequence of server Initial packets, not just one. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-56` | informative | The payload of an Initial packet contains CRYPTO frame(s) with a handshake message, ACK frames, or both. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The payload of an Initial packet includes a CRYPTO frame (or frames) containing a cryptographic handshake message, ACK frames, or both. | quic/frame.go + send.go: frames placed in Initial packets limited to CRYPTO/ACK plus those in fact -57. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-57` | informative | PING, PADDING, and CONNECTION_CLOSE frames of type 0x1c are also permitted in Initial packets (0x1d application close is not). <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | PING, PADDING, and CONNECTION_CLOSE frames of type 0x1c are also permitted. | quic/frame.go + close.go: Initial-space frame whitelist is CRYPTO, ACK, PING, PADDING, CONNECTION_CLOSE 0x1c only; 0x1d excluded. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-58` | informative | An endpoint receiving an Initial packet containing frames outside the permitted set may either discard the packet as spurious or treat it as a connection error. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that receives an Initial packet containing other frames can either discard the packet as spurious or treat it as a connection error. | quic/frame.go: receive-side validation of frame types allowed per packet number space; disallowed frame in Initial -> drop or conn error. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-59` | informative | The first packet a client sends always includes a CRYPTO frame containing the start or all of the first cryptographic handshake message. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The first packet sent by a client always includes a CRYPTO frame that contains the start or all of the first cryptographic handshake message. | quic/initial.go + send.go: first flight builder always emits CRYPTO with ClientHello in the first Initial. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-60` | informative | The first CRYPTO frame sent always begins at offset 0. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The first CRYPTO frame sent always begins at an offset of 0 | quic/frame_write.go + initial.go: initial CRYPTO frame offset field is 0. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-61` | informative | If the server sends a TLS HelloRetryRequest, the client sends another series of Initial packets whose CRYPTO frames continue at an offset matching the size of the first flight's CRYPTO data. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | will contain CRYPTO frames starting at an offset matching the size of the CRYPTO frames sent in the first flight of Initial packets | quic/initial.go: HRR handling continues the Initial CRYPTO offset stream, not reset to 0 (contrast with post-Retry restart). |
| `17.1-17.2.2-62` | informative | A client stops both sending and processing Initial packets once it sends its first Handshake packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client stops both sending and processing Initial packets when it sends its first Handshake packet. | quic/send.go + initial.go: on first Handshake packet sent, client discards Initial keys and ignores/ceases Initial traffic. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-63` | informative | A server stops sending and processing Initial packets when it receives its first Handshake packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server stops sending and processing Initial packets when it receives its first Handshake packet. | Server-side rule; client implication (server won't process late client Initials) needs no dedicated code in quic/. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-64` | informative | When Initial packets are abandoned, Initial packet protection keys are discarded along with any Initial-space loss recovery and congestion control state. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Initial packet protection keys are discarded (see Section 4.9.1 of [QUIC-TLS]) along with any loss recovery and congestion control state | quic/initial.go + send.go + ack_tracker.go: Initial key discard also drops Initial-space sent-packet tracking, loss timers, and in-flight byte counts. |
| `17.1-17.2.2-65` | informative | Any data in Initial-space CRYPTO frames is discarded and no longer retransmitted once Initial keys are discarded. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Any data in CRYPTO frames is discarded -- and no longer retransmitted -- when Initial keys are discarded. | quic/send.go + initial.go: retransmission queue for Initial CRYPTO data purged at key discard; no post-discard resends. |

### 9000 section 17.2.4-17.4 - Handshake packet, Retry packet (sending/handling/continuing), 1-RTT short header packets, latency spin bit

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `17.2.4-17.4-1` | informative | A Handshake packet is a long-header packet with type value 0x02, followed by the Length and Packet Number fields. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | A Handshake packet uses long headers with a type value of 0x02, followed by the Length and Packet Number fields | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: long-header type dispatch maps type 2 to Handshake; emitter writes type bits = 2 with Length + PN. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-2` | informative | The first byte of a Handshake packet contains the Reserved bits and Packet Number Length bits (same layout as other long-header protected packets). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The first byte contains the Reserved and Packet Number Length bits | quic/packet.go: Handshake first-byte parsing shares reserved-bit and pn-length handling with Initial path. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-3` | informative | After receiving a Handshake packet from the server, the client uses Handshake packets for its subsequent cryptographic handshake messages and acknowledgments. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Once a client has received a Handshake packet from a server, it uses Handshake packets to send subsequent cryptographic handshake messages and acknowledgments to the server. | quic/send.go: once Handshake keys are installed, client crypto flights and ACKs move to the Handshake PN space. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-4` | informative | In a Handshake packet the Destination Connection ID is the recipient-chosen connection ID and the Source Connection ID is the sender's wished connection ID. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The Destination Connection ID field in a Handshake packet contains a connection ID that is chosen by the recipient of the packet | quic/connid.go: Handshake packets carry server-issued DCID and our SCID, same selection as Initial after server SCID learned. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-5` | informative | Handshake packets have their own packet number space; the server's first Handshake packet has packet number 0. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Handshake packets have their own packet number space, and thus the first Handshake packet sent by a server contains a packet number of 0. | quic/ack_tracker.go + send.go: separate Handshake PN space for send and receive; decode expects server PN starting at 0. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-6` | informative | A Handshake packet's payload contains CRYPTO frames and may also contain PING, PADDING, or ACK frames. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The payload of this packet contains CRYPTO frames and could contain PING, PADDING, or ACK frames. | quic/frame.go: allowed frame set for the Handshake packet type (CRYPTO, PING, PADDING, ACK). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-7` | MAY | Handshake packets may carry CONNECTION_CLOSE frames, but only of type 0x1c. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Handshake packets MAY contain CONNECTION_CLOSE frames of type 0x1c. | quic/close.go + frame.go: when closing during handshake, emit CONNECTION_CLOSE 0x1c (never 0x1d) in Handshake packets; accept 0x1c inbound. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-8` | MUST | Receipt of a Handshake packet containing any frame other than CRYPTO/PING/PADDING/ACK/CONNECTION_CLOSE(0x1c) is a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MUST treat receipt of Handshake packets with other frames as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. | quic/frame.go + errors.go: per-packet-type frame whitelist for Handshake; violation closes connection with PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-9` | informative | CRYPTO data carried in Handshake packets is discarded and no longer retransmitted once Handshake protection keys are discarded. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | data in CRYPTO frames for Handshake packets is discarded -- and no longer retransmitted -- when Handshake protection keys are discarded | quic/send.go: on Handshake key discard, drop unacked Handshake-space CRYPTO from the retransmission set. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-10` | informative | A Retry packet is a long-header packet with type value 0x03 that carries a server-created address validation token. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | a Retry packet uses a long packet header with a type value of 0x03. It carries an address validation token created by the server. | quic/packet.go + retry.go: long-header type 3 parses as Retry (no Length, no Packet Number fields). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-11` | informative | In the Retry header the four bits after the packet type are an Unused field (not Reserved/PN-Length), followed by Version and the two connection ID fields. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Long Packet Type (2) = 3, Unused (4), Version (32), | quic/packet.go: Retry parser treats low nibble of byte 0 as unused, then Version, DCID len/DCID, SCID len/SCID. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-12` | informative | After the connection ID fields, a Retry packet contains a variable-length Retry Token followed by a 128-bit Retry Integrity Tag as the final 16 bytes. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Retry Token (..), Retry Integrity Tag (128), | quic/retry.go: token = remaining bytes minus last 16; last 16 bytes are the integrity tag. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-13` | informative | A Retry packet has no protected fields (no header or packet protection to remove). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | A Retry packet does not contain any protected fields. | quic/retry.go: Retry processed without header-protection removal or AEAD decryption; only the integrity tag AEAD check applies. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-14` | MUST | The client must ignore the Unused bits of a Retry packet, which the server sets to an arbitrary value. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The value in the Unused field is set to an arbitrary value by the server; a client MUST ignore these bits. | quic/retry.go: do not validate or branch on byte0 & 0x0f of a Retry packet. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-15` | informative | The Retry Integrity Tag is computed as defined in Section 5.8 of RFC 9001 (QUIC-TLS). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Retry Integrity Tag: Defined in Section 5.8 ("Retry Packet Integrity") of [QUIC-TLS]. | quic/retry.go: integrity tag verified with the RFC 9001 5.8 fixed key/nonce AEAD over the Retry pseudo-packet. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-16` | informative | The server sets the Retry packet's Destination Connection ID to the connection ID the client used as Source Connection ID in its Initial packet. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The server populates the Destination Connection ID with the connection ID that the client included in the Source Connection ID of the Initial packet. | quic/retry.go: client can sanity-check that Retry DCID equals the SCID it sent in its Initial before processing. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-17` | MUST NOT | The Source Connection ID a server puts in a Retry must not equal the Destination Connection ID of the client's packet. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | This value MUST NOT be equal to the Destination Connection ID field of the packet sent by the client. | Server-side rule; client-side enforcement is the discard rule (fact 18). Test peer in quic/retry.go tests should honor it. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-18` | MUST | The client must discard a Retry packet whose Source Connection ID equals the Destination Connection ID of the client's Initial packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST discard a Retry packet that contains a Source Connection ID field that is identical to the Destination Connection ID field of its Initial packet. | quic/retry.go: compare Retry SCID against the original DCID chosen for the Initial; discard on equality. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-19` | MUST | The client must use the Retry packet's Source Connection ID as the Destination Connection ID of all subsequent packets it sends. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client MUST use the value from the Source Connection ID field of the Retry packet in the Destination Connection ID field of subsequent packets that it sends. | quic/retry.go + connid.go: adopt Retry SCID as the active DCID for every later packet (Initial, Handshake, 1-RTT). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-20` | MAY | A server may send Retry packets in response to Initial and 0-RTT packets (possibly multiple across datagrams). <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server MAY send Retry packets in response to Initial and 0-RTT packets. | quic/retry.go: client must be prepared for a Retry arriving in response to its first flight at any pre-handshake point. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-21` | MUST NOT | A server must not send more than one Retry packet in response to a single UDP datagram. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST NOT send more than one Retry packet in response to a single UDP datagram. | Server-only; client already processes at most one Retry per attempt (fact 22). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-22` | MUST | A client must accept and process at most one Retry packet per connection attempt. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST accept and process at most one Retry packet for each connection attempt. | quic/retry.go: retry-processed flag on the connection; second Retry is dropped. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-23` | MUST | Once the client has processed an Initial or a Retry packet from the server, it must discard any subsequent Retry packets. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | After the client has received and processed an Initial or Retry packet from the server, it MUST discard any subsequent Retry packets that it receives. | quic/retry.go: discard Retry also after any server Initial has been processed, not just after a prior Retry. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-24` | MUST | The client must discard any Retry packet whose Retry Integrity Tag fails validation. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients MUST discard Retry packets that have a Retry Integrity Tag that cannot be validated | quic/retry.go: verify integrity tag (RFC 9001 5.8) before acting on the Retry; silent drop on failure. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-25` | MUST | The client must discard a Retry packet with a zero-length Retry Token field. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST discard a Retry packet with a zero-length Retry Token field. | quic/retry.go: reject/drop Retry whose token length is 0 (payload length == 16-byte tag only). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-26` | informative | The client continues connection establishment by sending an Initial packet that includes the Retry-provided token. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client responds to a Retry packet with an Initial packet that includes the provided Retry token to continue connection establishment. | quic/retry.go + initial.go: resend the Initial flight with the Token field carrying the Retry token. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-27` | informative | Changing the Destination Connection ID after Retry changes the keys used to protect subsequent Initial packets (Initial secrets re-derived from the new DCID). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Changing the Destination Connection ID field also results in a change to the keys used to protect the Initial packet. | quic/initial.go: re-derive Initial secrets/keys from the Retry SCID before sending the post-Retry Initial. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-28` | informative | The client sets the Token field of the post-Retry Initial packet to the token provided in the Retry packet. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | It also sets the Token field to the token provided in the Retry packet. | quic/packet_write.go + initial.go: Initial header Token field populated from stored Retry token. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-29` | MUST NOT | The client must not change its Source Connection ID after a Retry, since the server may bind it into token validation. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The client MUST NOT change the Source Connection ID because the server could include the connection ID as part of its token validation logic | quic/retry.go + connid.go: keep the original client SCID in all post-Retry Initials. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-30` | informative | A Retry packet carries no packet number and cannot be acknowledged by the client. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A Retry packet does not include a packet number and cannot be explicitly acknowledged by a client. | quic/ack_tracker.go: Retry must never create ACK-eliciting/receive state; no PN is decoded from it. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-31` | informative | The client uses the Retry-copied Destination Connection ID until it receives a server Initial packet carrying an updated Source Connection ID. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | copies the Source Connection ID field from the Retry packet to the Destination Connection ID field and uses this value until an Initial packet with an updated value is received | quic/connid.go: DCID replaced again from the server Initial's SCID per Section 7.2, after the Retry adoption. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-32` | informative | The Retry token value is copied into every subsequent Initial packet the client sends. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | The value of the Token field is copied to all subsequent Initial packets | quic/initial.go: token attached to all later Initials, including retransmissions and split CRYPTO flights. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-33` | informative | Apart from the updated Destination Connection ID and Token fields, the post-Retry Initial is subject to the same restrictions as the first Initial (padding, frame limits, etc.). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | the Initial packet sent by the client is subject to the same restrictions as the first Initial packet | quic/initial.go: post-Retry Initial still padded to the 1200-byte minimum datagram and frame-restricted like the first. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-34` | MUST | The client must send the same cryptographic handshake message in the post-Retry Initial as in the original Initial. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST use the same cryptographic handshake message it included in this packet. | quic/retry.go: reuse the identical ClientHello bytes (no TLS re-generation) when rebuilding the Initial after Retry. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-35` | MAY | A server may treat a post-Retry packet containing a different cryptographic handshake message as a connection error or discard it. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY treat a packet that contains a different cryptographic handshake message as a connection error or discard it. | Server-side consequence of fact 34; interop risk only if client regenerates its ClientHello. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-36` | informative | Adding the Token field shrinks the room for the cryptographic handshake message, possibly forcing the client to send multiple Initial packets. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | including a Token field reduces the available space for the cryptographic handshake message, which might result in the client needing to send multiple Initial packets | quic/initial.go: CRYPTO data must be splittable across several Initials when the token consumes payload space. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-37` | MAY | A client may attempt 0-RTT after a Retry by sending 0-RTT packets to the server-provided connection ID. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MAY attempt 0-RTT after receiving a Retry packet by sending 0-RTT packets to the connection ID provided by the server. | N/A for this client (0-RTT is an explicit non-goal); no code path needed. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-38` | MUST NOT | The client must not reset the packet number of any packet number space after processing a Retry packet (same key+nonce on different content would break packet protection). <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT reset the packet number for any packet number space after processing a Retry packet. | quic/send.go: per-space next-PN counters continue monotonically across Retry; verify the post-Retry Initial does not restart at 0 if an Initial was already sent. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-39` | MAY | A server may abort the connection if it detects the client reset its packet number after Retry. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MAY abort the connection if it detects that the client reset the packet number. | Server-side consequence of fact 38. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-40` | informative | The connection IDs used in Initial and Retry exchanges are echoed in transport parameters and validated per Section 7.3 (original_destination_connection_id / retry_source_connection_id). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The connection IDs used in Initial and Retry packets exchanged between client and server are copied to the transport parameters and validated as described in Section 7.3. | quic/transport_params.go: client validates original_destination_connection_id and (when Retry occurred) retry_source_connection_id against the observed exchange. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-41` | informative | QUIC v1 defines exactly one packet type using the short packet header (1-RTT). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This version of QUIC defines a single packet type that uses the short packet header. | quic/packet.go: any short-header packet is parsed as 1-RTT; no other short types exist. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-42` | informative | 1-RTT packets use the short header and are used only after version and 1-RTT keys are negotiated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | It is used after the version and 1-RTT keys are negotiated. | quic/send.go: 1-RTT packets emitted only once 1-RTT keys are installed. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-43` | informative | 1-RTT packet layout: form=0, fixed=1, spin(1), reserved(2), key phase(1), pn-length(2), then DCID (no length byte), Packet Number (8..32), Payload. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Header Form (1) = 0, Fixed Bit (1) = 1, Spin Bit (1), Reserved Bits (2), Key Phase (1), Packet Number Length (2), | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: short-header byte-0 bit layout and field order for parse/emit. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-44` | informative | The most significant bit (0x80) of byte 0 is 0 for the short header. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The most significant bit (0x80) of byte 0 is set to 0 for the short header. | quic/packet.go: header-form dispatch keys on byte0 & 0x80. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-45` | informative | The Fixed Bit (0x40) of a short-header packet is set to 1 when sending. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | The next bit (0x40) of byte 0 is set to 1. | quic/packet_write.go: OR 0x40 into short-header byte 0. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-46` | MUST | Short-header packets with a zero Fixed Bit are not valid in this version and must be discarded. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | Packets containing a zero value for this bit are not valid packets in this version and MUST be discarded. | quic/packet.go: drop (not connection-error) inbound short-header packets with byte0 & 0x40 == 0. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-47` | informative | The third most significant bit (0x20) of byte 0 in a 1-RTT packet is the latency spin bit, set per Section 17.4. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The third most significant bit (0x20) of byte 0 is the latency spin bit, set as described in Section 17.4. | quic/packet_write.go: spin bit position 0x20 in the short-header first byte. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-48` | MUST | The two Reserved Bits (mask 0x18) of a 1-RTT packet must be 0 prior to header protection; they are covered by header protection. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | The value included prior to protection MUST be set to 0. | quic/packet_write.go: never set bits 0x18 in the plaintext short-header first byte before header protection is applied. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-49` | MUST | Receipt of a 1-RTT packet with non-zero reserved bits, after removing both packet and header protection, is a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MUST treat receipt of a packet that has a non-zero value for these bits, after removing both packet and header protection, as a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION | quic/packet.go + errors.go: after successful AEAD decryption, check byte0 & 0x18 != 0 and close with PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-50` | informative | The reserved-bit check must happen only after full decryption; discarding based on reserved bits after only header-protection removal exposes the endpoint to attacks. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Discarding such a packet after only removing header protection can expose the endpoint to attacks | quic/packet.go: verify ordering — payload AEAD open succeeds first, reserved-bit check second. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-51` | informative | The Key Phase bit (0x04) of byte 0 identifies the packet protection keys in use; it is protected by header protection. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The next bit (0x04) of byte 0 indicates the key phase, which allows a recipient of a packet to identify the packet protection keys | quic/packet.go: key phase bit read after header-protection removal drives RFC 9001 key-update detection. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-52` | informative | The Packet Number Length bits (mask 0x03) encode the PN field length minus one, i.e. PN length in bytes = (byte0 & 0x03) + 1; header-protected. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | encoded as an unsigned two-bit integer that is one less than the length of the Packet Number field in bytes | quic/packet.go + packet_write.go: pnLen = (b0 & 0x03) + 1 on decode; encode writes pnLen-1. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-53` | informative | The short-header Destination Connection ID is the connection ID chosen by the intended recipient; there is no length byte on the wire. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The Destination Connection ID is a connection ID that is chosen by the intended recipient of the packet. | quic/connid.go + packet.go: short-header DCID length is implicit — client must slice it using the length of the CIDs it issued. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-54` | informative | The 1-RTT Packet Number field is 1 to 4 bytes long, protected by header protection, with its length given by the Packet Number Length bits. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The Packet Number field is 1 to 4 bytes long. The packet number is protected using header protection | quic/packet.go: PN sampling/decode supports 1..4 byte PNs under header protection (Section 17.1 window algorithm). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-55` | informative | 1-RTT packets always carry a 1-RTT-protected payload (minimum payload present). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 1-RTT packets always include a 1-RTT protected payload. | quic/packet_write.go: never emit a 1-RTT packet with an empty plaintext payload. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-56` | informative | Only the header form bit and the Destination Connection ID of a short-header packet are version-independent; all other fields are version-specific. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The header form bit and the Destination Connection ID field of a short header packet are version independent. | quic/packet.go: do not interpret version-specific short-header bits before confirming QUIC v1. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-57` | informative | Spin bit mechanics: the server reflects the received spin value; the client inverts (spins) it after one RTT, enabling passive RTT measurement. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | The server reflects the spin value received, while the client "spins" it after one RTT. | quic/send.go / packet_write.go: overview of the roles; client-specific rule is fact 69. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-58` | OPTIONAL | The latency spin bit is an OPTIONAL feature of QUIC v1. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The spin bit is an OPTIONAL feature of this version of QUIC. | quic/: spin-bit support may be omitted entirely, provided the disabled-mode rules (facts 62-64) are followed. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-59` | MUST | An endpoint that does not support the spin bit must disable it as defined (random/ignored bit behavior). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that does not support this feature MUST disable it, as defined below. | quic/packet_write.go: if the client does not implement spinning, it must follow the disabled-mode rules — check what value it writes at 0x20 and that it never acts on inbound spin. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-60` | MUST | Implementations must let administrators disable the spin bit globally or per connection. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Implementations MUST allow administrators of clients and servers to disable the spin bit either globally or on a per-connection basis. | quic/: if spin is ever enabled, expose a config knob (Options struct) to disable it; trivially satisfied if spin is never implemented as enabled. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-61` | MUST | Even when not administratively disabled, endpoints must randomly disable the spin bit for at least one in every 16 network paths or one in every 16 connection IDs. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | endpoints MUST disable their use of the spin bit for a random selection of at least one in every 16 network paths, or for one in every 16 connection IDs | quic/: applies only if spin is implemented as enabled — per-connection 1/16 random disable at setup. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-62` | MAY | When the spin bit is disabled, an endpoint may set the outgoing spin bit to any value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | When the spin bit is disabled, endpoints MAY set the spin bit to any value | quic/packet_write.go: disabled-mode spin value is unconstrained (see fact 64 for the RECOMMENDED choice). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-63` | MUST | When the spin bit is disabled, an endpoint must ignore any incoming spin bit value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | endpoints MAY set the spin bit to any value and MUST ignore any incoming value | quic/packet.go: inbound byte0 & 0x20 must not affect any state when spin is disabled/unimplemented. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-64` | RECOMMENDED | It is recommended that a disabled spin bit be set to a random value chosen independently per packet or per connection ID. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | It is RECOMMENDED that endpoints set the spin bit to a random value either chosen independently for each packet or chosen independently for each connection ID. | quic/packet_write.go: check whether the client writes a constant 0 at 0x20 vs the recommended random value (per-connection random is cheapest). |
| `17.2.4-17.4-65` | informative | If the spin bit is enabled, the endpoint keeps a per-network-path spin value and writes the stored value into each 1-RTT packet sent on that path. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | the endpoint maintains a spin value for each network path and sets the spin bit in the packet header to the currently stored value when a 1-RTT packet is sent | quic/send.go: single path (no migration), so one spin value per connection if implemented. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-66` | informative | The spin value is initialized to 0 for each network path. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The spin value is initialized to 0 in the endpoint for each network path. | quic/: spin state starts at 0 at connection establishment if implemented. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-67` | informative | Each endpoint also tracks the highest packet number seen from its peer on each path, used to gate spin updates. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Each endpoint also remembers the highest packet number seen from its peer on each path. | quic/ack_tracker.go: highest received 1-RTT PN already tracked for ACK/PN-decode; spin update (if implemented) reuses it. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-68` | informative | Server rule: on receiving a 1-RTT packet that raises the highest packet number seen from the client on a path, the server sets its spin value equal to the received spin bit. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | it sets the spin value for that path to be equal to the spin bit in the received packet | Server-side reflection rule; not needed in the client. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-69` | informative | Client rule: on receiving a 1-RTT packet that raises the highest packet number seen from the server on a path, the client sets its spin value to the inverse of the received spin bit. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | it sets the spin value for that path to the inverse of the spin bit in the received packet | quic/packet.go + send.go: only if spin is implemented — update spin = !recvSpin on new highest 1-RTT PN. |
| `17.2.4-17.4-70` | informative | An endpoint resets the spin value for a path to 0 when it changes the connection ID used on that path. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint resets the spin value for a network path to 0 when changing the connection ID being used on that network path. | quic/connid.go: if spin is implemented, reset spin state when switching active DCID (NEW_CONNECTION_ID adoption). |

### 9000 section 18 - Transport Parameter Encoding

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `18-1` | informative | QUIC transport parameters are carried in the extension_data field of the quic_transport_parameters TLS extension, encoded as a back-to-back sequence of transport parameters. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The extension_data field of the quic_transport_parameters extension defined in [QUIC-TLS] contains the QUIC transport parameters. | quic/transport_params.go — verify marshal/unmarshal targets the quic_transport_parameters extension body as a flat sequence with no outer length wrapper. |
| `18-2` | informative | Each transport parameter is encoded as a (Transport Parameter ID, Transport Parameter Length, Transport Parameter Value) tuple where ID and Length are variable-length integers. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Each transport parameter is encoded as an (identifier, length, value) tuple, as shown in Figure 21 | quic/transport_params.go — check encoder/decoder uses varint ID + varint length + raw value per Figure 21 (internal/bytesx varint codec). |
| `18-3` | informative | The Transport Parameter Length field gives the length of the Transport Parameter Value field in bytes. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The Transport Parameter Length field contains the length of the Transport Parameter Value field in bytes. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must bound each value read by the declared length and reject truncated values. |
| `18-4` | informative | Transport parameter identifiers of the form 31*N+27 are reserved greasing values with no semantics that may carry arbitrary values; they exercise the rule that unknown transport parameters are ignored. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Transport parameters with an identifier of the form "31 * N + 27" for integer values of N are reserved to exercise the requirement that unknown transport parameters be ignored. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must skip unknown/reserved IDs (any value, any length) without error; optionally emit a greasing parameter. |
| `18-5` | informative | Transport parameters identified as integers are encoded as variable-length integers (Section 16) inside the value field. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Those transport parameters that are identified as integers use a variable-length integer encoding; see Section 16. | quic/transport_params.go — integer-valued params decode value as varint (via internal/bytesx) and the varint must consume exactly the declared length. |
| `18-6` | informative | An absent transport parameter has a default value of 0 unless its definition states another default. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Transport parameters have a default value of 0 if the transport parameter is absent, unless otherwise stated. | quic/transport_params.go — verify zero defaults for absent params, with explicit overrides only for max_udp_payload_size (65527), ack_delay_exponent (3), max_ack_delay (25), active_connection_id_limit (2). |
| `18-7` | informative | original_destination_connection_id (0x00) carries the Destination Connection ID from the client's first Initial packet and is only sent by a server. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | This parameter is the value of the Destination Connection ID field from the first Initial packet sent by the client | quic/transport_params.go + retry.go — client must parse 0x00 and validate it equals the DCID of its first Initial (Section 7.3 authentication). |
| `18-8` | informative | max_idle_timeout (0x01) is an integer value in milliseconds. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The maximum idle timeout is a value in milliseconds that is encoded as an integer | quic/transport_params.go — check 0x01 encodes/decodes as varint milliseconds and feeds the connection idle timer. |
| `18-9` | informative | Idle timeout is disabled only when both endpoints omit max_idle_timeout or advertise a value of 0. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Idle timeout is disabled when both endpoints omit this transport parameter or specify a value of 0. | quic/transport_params.go + close.go — verify effective idle timeout combines both endpoints' values and 0/absent means that endpoint imposes no timeout. |
| `18-10` | informative | stateless_reset_token (0x02) is a 16-byte value used to verify a stateless reset. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This parameter is a sequence of 16 bytes. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must require exactly 16 bytes for 0x02 and store the token for reset detection (close.go). |
| `18-11` | MUST NOT | A client is forbidden from sending the stateless_reset_token transport parameter; a server may send it. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | This transport parameter MUST NOT be sent by a client but MAY be sent by a server. | quic/transport_params.go — confirm the client encoder can never emit ID 0x02. |
| `18-12` | informative | A server that omits stateless_reset_token cannot use stateless reset for the connection ID negotiated during the handshake. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server that does not send this transport parameter cannot use stateless reset (Section 10.3) for the connection ID negotiated during the handshake. | quic/close.go — client should only match incoming datagrams against a handshake-CID reset token if the server actually sent 0x02. |
| `18-13` | informative | max_udp_payload_size (0x03) is an integer limiting the size of UDP payloads the endpoint is willing to receive; larger datagrams are unlikely to be processed. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | an integer value that limits the size of UDP payloads that the endpoint is willing to receive | quic/transport_params.go + send.go — sender must cap outgoing datagram size at the peer's advertised max_udp_payload_size. |
| `18-14` | informative | max_udp_payload_size defaults to 65527 when absent, and any advertised value below 1200 is invalid (a TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR per Section 7.4). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The default for this parameter is the maximum permitted UDP payload of 65527. Values below 1200 are invalid. | quic/transport_params.go — verify default 65527 and rejection of values < 1200 on decode; initial.go for interaction with the 1200-byte minimum datagram size. |
| `18-15` | informative | max_udp_payload_size constrains datagram size in addition to the path MTU, but is a property of the endpoint, not the path. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This limit does act as an additional constraint on datagram size in the same way as the path MTU | quic/initial.go + send.go — effective datagram limit must be min(path MTU estimate, peer max_udp_payload_size). |
| `18-16` | informative | initial_max_data (0x04) is the initial connection-level flow control limit, equivalent to a MAX_DATA frame sent immediately after handshake completion. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This is equivalent to sending a MAX_DATA (Section 19.9) for the connection immediately after completing the handshake. | quic/transport_params.go + recvflow.go/send.go — peer's 0x04 seeds the connection send window; our 0x04 seeds the advertised receive window. |
| `18-17` | informative | initial_max_stream_data_bidi_local (0x05) is the initial flow control limit for bidirectional streams opened by the endpoint that sends the parameter; in client transport parameters it governs stream IDs with LSBs 0x00, in server transport parameters LSBs 0x01. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | In client transport parameters, this applies to streams with an identifier with the least significant two bits set to 0x00 | quic/transport_params.go + stream.go — server's 0x05 must seed the send limit on server-initiated bidi streams (LSB 0x01) that the client writes to; client's own 0x05 advertises receive credit on its client-initiated bidi streams. |
| `18-18` | informative | initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote (0x06) is the initial flow control limit for bidirectional streams opened by the endpoint that receives the parameter; in client transport parameters it governs LSBs 0x01, in server transport parameters LSBs 0x00. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This limit applies to newly created bidirectional streams opened by the endpoint that receives the transport parameter. | quic/transport_params.go + stream.go — server's 0x06 seeds the client's send limit on client-initiated bidi streams (LSB 0x00): the critical request-path mapping; verify 0x05/0x06 are not swapped. |
| `18-19` | informative | initial_max_stream_data_uni (0x07) is the initial flow control limit for unidirectional streams opened by the endpoint that receives the parameter; in client transport parameters it governs LSBs 0x03, in server transport parameters LSBs 0x02. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | In client transport parameters, this applies to streams with an identifier with the least significant two bits set to 0x03 | quic/transport_params.go + stream.go — server's 0x07 seeds the client's send limit on client-initiated uni streams (LSB 0x02); client's 0x07 advertises credit for server-initiated uni streams (LSB 0x03). |
| `18-20` | informative | initial_max_streams_bidi (0x08) sets the initial number of bidirectional streams the receiving endpoint may initiate, equivalent to a MAX_STREAMS frame of the corresponding type with the same value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Setting this parameter is equivalent to sending a MAX_STREAMS (Section 19.11) of the corresponding type with the same value. | quic/transport_params.go + recvflow.go — server's 0x08 seeds how many bidi streams the client may open before needing MAX_STREAMS. |
| `18-21` | informative | If initial_max_streams_bidi is absent or zero, the peer cannot open any bidirectional streams until a MAX_STREAMS frame raises the limit. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If this parameter is absent or zero, the peer cannot open bidirectional streams until a MAX_STREAMS frame is sent. | quic/stream.go + recvflow.go — client stream-open must block/fail at limit 0 and resume on MAX_STREAMS from the server. |
| `18-22` | informative | initial_max_streams_uni (0x09) sets the initial number of unidirectional streams the receiving endpoint may initiate, equivalent to a MAX_STREAMS frame of the corresponding type. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | contains the initial maximum number of unidirectional streams the endpoint that receives this transport parameter is permitted to initiate | quic/transport_params.go + recvflow.go — server's 0x09 gates client-initiated uni streams (H3 control/QPACK streams depend on this being >= 3). |
| `18-23` | informative | If initial_max_streams_uni is absent or zero, the peer cannot open any unidirectional streams until a MAX_STREAMS frame is sent. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If this parameter is absent or zero, the peer cannot open unidirectional streams until a MAX_STREAMS frame is sent. | quic/stream.go + recvflow.go — verify uni stream-open honours a zero initial limit instead of assuming credit. |
| `18-24` | informative | ack_delay_exponent (0x0a) is the exponent used to decode the ACK Delay field of ACK frames; when absent, the default is 3 (a multiplier of 8). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If this value is absent, a default value of 3 is assumed (indicating a multiplier of 8). | quic/ack_tracker.go + transport_params.go — decode peer ACK Delay using the PEER's exponent (default 3); encode our ACK Delay using OUR advertised exponent. |
| `18-25` | informative | ack_delay_exponent values above 20 are invalid (TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR per Section 7.4). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Values above 20 are invalid. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must reject ack_delay_exponent > 20 with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. |
| `18-26` | SHOULD | The advertised max_ack_delay should include the endpoint's expected timer/alarm firing slop (e.g., a 5 ms timer that fires up to 1 ms late should be advertised as 6 ms). <br>_(endpoint,sender [CLIENT])_ | This value SHOULD include the receiver's expected delays in alarms firing. | quic/transport_params.go + ack_tracker.go — check our advertised max_ack_delay covers the actual ACK-delay timer granularity plus scheduling slop. |
| `18-27` | informative | max_ack_delay (0x0b) is in milliseconds and defaults to 25 milliseconds when absent. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If this value is absent, a default of 25 milliseconds is assumed. | quic/transport_params.go — verify default 25 ms; loss recovery (PTO) must use the peer's max_ack_delay (RFC 9002). |
| `18-28` | informative | max_ack_delay values of 2^14 or greater are invalid (TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR per Section 7.4). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Values of 2^14 or greater are invalid. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must reject max_ack_delay >= 16384 with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. |
| `18-29` | informative | disable_active_migration (0x0c) is included when the endpoint does not support active connection migration on the handshake address. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The disable active migration transport parameter is included if the endpoint does not support active connection migration (Section 9) on the address being used during the handshake. | quic/transport_params.go — this migration-free client may legitimately send 0x0c; verify both encode and accept-on-decode. |
| `18-30` | informative | disable_active_migration is a zero-length transport parameter (a flag with an empty value). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This parameter is a zero-length value. | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must accept length 0 for 0x0c and reject a non-empty value. |
| `18-31` | MUST NOT | An endpoint that receives disable_active_migration is forbidden from using a new local address when sending to the address the peer used during the handshake. <br>_(receiver,endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that receives this transport parameter MUST NOT use a new local address when sending to the address that the peer used during the handshake. | quic/send.go — client does no migration, so trivially satisfied; verify the UDP socket/local address is never rebound mid-connection. |
| `18-32` | informative | disable_active_migration does not prohibit migration after a client has acted on a preferred_address transport parameter. <br>_(client [not-client])_ | This transport parameter does not prohibit connection migration after a client has acted on a preferred_address transport parameter. | quic/transport_params.go — N/A for this client (no migration, preferred_address never acted on); no code needed. |
| `18-33` | informative | preferred_address (0x0d) is only sent by a server and is used to move the server address at the end of the handshake. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | The server's preferred address is used to effect a change in server address at the end of the handshake | quic/transport_params.go — client must be able to parse 0x0d (servers do send it) even though this client never migrates to it. |
| `18-34` | MAY | A server may advertise a preferred address for only one address family by sending an all-zero address and port (0.0.0.0:0 or [::]:0) for the other family. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | Servers MAY choose to only send a preferred address of one address family by sending an all-zero address and port (0.0.0.0:0 or [::]:0) for the other family. | quic/transport_params.go — parser must accept an all-zero IPv4 or IPv6 address/port pair inside 0x0d without error. |
| `18-35` | informative | preferred_address wire layout (Figure 22): IPv4 Address (4 bytes), IPv4 Port (2), IPv6 Address (16), IPv6 Port (2), Connection ID Length (1), Connection ID (variable), Stateless Reset Token (16). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The four-byte IPv4 Address field is followed by the associated two-byte IPv4 Port field. | quic/transport_params.go — verify 0x0d parser walks exactly 4+2+16+2+1+CIDLen+16 bytes and rejects trailing/short data. |
| `18-36` | informative | IP addresses inside preferred_address are encoded in network byte order. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | IP addresses are encoded in network byte order. | quic/transport_params.go — big-endian address/port parsing for 0x0d. |
| `18-37` | informative | The Connection ID and Stateless Reset Token in preferred_address are an alternative connection ID with sequence number 1, syntactically and semantically identical to NEW_CONNECTION_ID fields. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | contain an alternative connection ID that has a sequence number of 1 | quic/connid.go — if the client tracks peer CIDs, the preferred_address CID must occupy sequence number 1 (so a NEW_CONNECTION_ID with seq 1 is not treated as new) and counts against active_connection_id_limit. |
| `18-38` | MUST NOT | A server that chose a zero-length source connection ID is forbidden from providing a preferred_address at all. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | A server that chooses a zero-length connection ID MUST NOT provide a preferred address. | quic/transport_params.go — client validation: presence of 0x0d from a zero-length-SCID server is a protocol violation (see 18-40). |
| `18-39` | MUST NOT | A server is forbidden from including a zero-length Connection ID inside the preferred_address transport parameter. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | a server MUST NOT include a zero-length connection ID in this transport parameter | quic/transport_params.go — client parser must check Connection ID Length > 0 in 0x0d. |
| `18-40` | MUST | A client that receives a preferred_address violating the zero-length-connection-ID rules must treat it as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST treat a violation of these requirements as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. | quic/transport_params.go + errors.go — even though this client ignores preferred_address, it MUST still close with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR (0x8) on a zero-length CID in 0x0d or on 0x0d from a zero-length-SCID server. |
| `18-41` | informative | active_connection_id_limit (0x0e) is the maximum number of peer connection IDs the endpoint is willing to store. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | specifying the maximum number of connection IDs from the peer that an endpoint is willing to store | quic/transport_params.go + connid.go — our advertised 0x0e bounds how many NEW_CONNECTION_ID entries we accept before it is a CONNECTION_ID_LIMIT_ERROR (Section 5.1.1). |
| `18-42` | informative | The active_connection_id_limit count includes the connection ID received during the handshake, the one in preferred_address, and those received in NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This value includes the connection ID received during the handshake, that received in the preferred_address transport parameter, and those received in NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames. | quic/connid.go — verify the active-CID accounting counts the handshake CID and any preferred_address CID, not just NEW_CONNECTION_ID frames. |
| `18-43` | MUST | An advertised active_connection_id_limit value must be at least 2. <br>_(sender,endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The value of the active_connection_id_limit parameter MUST be at least 2. | quic/transport_params.go — client encoder must never advertise 0x0e < 2 (omit it or send >= 2). |
| `18-44` | MUST | An endpoint receiving an active_connection_id_limit less than 2 must close the connection with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(receiver,endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint that receives a value less than 2 MUST close the connection with an error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. | quic/transport_params.go + errors.go — decoder must reject 0x0e values 0 and 1 with TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR (0x8). |
| `18-45` | informative | active_connection_id_limit defaults to 2 when absent. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If this transport parameter is absent, a default of 2 is assumed. | quic/transport_params.go — verify default 2, an exception to the general default-0 rule. |
| `18-46` | informative | An endpoint that issues zero-length connection IDs never sends NEW_CONNECTION_ID and therefore ignores the active_connection_id_limit it received from its peer. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If an endpoint issues a zero-length connection ID, it will never send a NEW_CONNECTION_ID frame and therefore ignores the active_connection_id_limit value received from its peer. | quic/connid.go — if this client uses a zero-length source CID, the peer's 0x0e can be ignored on the send side (client sends no NEW_CONNECTION_ID). |
| `18-47` | informative | initial_source_connection_id (0x0f) carries the value the endpoint placed in the Source Connection ID field of the first Initial packet it sends. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This is the value that the endpoint included in the Source Connection ID field of the first Initial packet it sends for the connection | quic/transport_params.go + initial.go — client must send its own 0x0f matching its first Initial SCID and validate the server's 0x0f against the server's Initial SCID (Section 7.3). |
| `18-48` | informative | retry_source_connection_id (0x10) carries the Source Connection ID the server used in its Retry packet and is only sent by a server. <br>_(server [CLIENT])_ | This is the value that the server included in the Source Connection ID field of a Retry packet | quic/retry.go + transport_params.go — after a Retry, client must validate 0x10 equals the Retry packet's SCID; absent-after-Retry or present-without-Retry is a TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR (Section 7.3). |
| `18-49` | informative | The three initial per-stream flow control transport parameters, when present, are equivalent to sending MAX_STREAM_DATA on every stream of the corresponding type immediately after it opens. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | are equivalent to sending a MAX_STREAM_DATA frame (Section 19.10) on every stream of the corresponding type immediately after opening | quic/stream.go — every newly opened stream must be seeded with the matching initial_max_stream_data_* value as its send/receive limit. |
| `18-50` | informative | If an initial_max_stream_data_* transport parameter is absent, streams of that type start with a flow control limit of 0. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If the transport parameter is absent, streams of that type start with a flow control limit of 0. | quic/stream.go — absent parameter must yield send limit 0 (writer blocks until MAX_STREAM_DATA), not some assumed default. |
| `18-51` | MUST NOT | A client is forbidden from including any server-only transport parameter: original_destination_connection_id, preferred_address, retry_source_connection_id, or stateless_reset_token. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT include any server-only transport parameter: original_destination_connection_id, preferred_address, retry_source_connection_id, or stateless_reset_token. | quic/transport_params.go — client encoder must be structurally unable to emit IDs 0x00, 0x02, 0x0d, 0x10. |
| `18-52` | MUST | A server must treat receipt of any client-forbidden (server-only) transport parameter as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | A server MUST treat receipt of any of these transport parameters as a connection error of type TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR. | quic/transport_params.go — server-side enforcement only; relevant to the in-repo test peer if it validates client transport parameters. |

### 9000 section 19.1-19.8 - RFC 9000 Frame Types and Formats: PADDING, PING, ACK (ranges, ECN counts), RESET_STREAM, STOP_SENDING, CRYPTO, NEW_TOKEN, STREAM

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `19.1-19.8-1` | informative | A PADDING frame is type 0x00 with no content: it is exactly the single type byte and has no semantic value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | a PADDING frame consists of the single byte that identifies the frame as a PADDING frame | quic/frame.go + frame_write.go: type 0x00 parses/encodes as a lone byte with zero payload. |
