| `21.2-21.14-6` | informative | Because addresses cannot change during the handshake, endpoints can discard packets received on a different network path during the handshake. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Addresses cannot change during the handshake, so endpoints can discard packets that are received on a different network path. | quic dial/read path (send.go): verify the client uses a connected UDP socket so packets from other source addresses are rejected by the kernel. |
| `21.2-21.14-7` | informative | Endpoints are permitted to use additional, unspecified methods to detect and recover from handshake interference; no specific method is mandated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints are permitted to use other methods to detect and attempt to recover from interference with the handshake. | Discretionary; no action required in quic/ — handshake tamper detection is delegated to the TLS layer (RFC 9001). |
| `21.2-21.14-8` | SHOULD | Servers should mitigate the amplification attack against released addresses by limiting the usage and lifetime of address validation tokens. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Servers SHOULD provide mitigations for this attack by limiting the usage and lifetime of address validation tokens | Server-only; quic/retry.go only echoes Retry tokens back — no client action. |
| `21.2-21.14-9` | MAY | An endpoint may deliberately skip packet numbers when sending in order to detect a peer that acknowledges packets it never received (optimistic ACK attack). <br>_(endpoint,sender [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint MAY skip packet numbers when sending packets to detect this behavior. | quic/send.go packet-number allocation: skipping is optional and unimplemented; verify ACK processing (ack handling in recovery) already errors on acknowledgment of a never-sent packet number. |
| `21.2-21.14-10` | informative | On detecting an optimistic ACK (acknowledgment of a skipped/unsent packet number), the endpoint can immediately close the connection with PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint can then immediately close the connection with a connection error of type PROTOCOL_VIOLATION | quic/errors.go (PROTOCOL_VIOLATION) + close.go: verify an ACK covering an unsent packet number triggers connection close with PROTOCOL_VIOLATION. |
| `21.2-21.14-11` | SHOULD NOT | QUIC servers should not be deployed in networks lacking ingress filtering that also contain inadequately secured UDP endpoints, because spoofed-migration forgery has no adequate countermeasure. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | QUIC servers SHOULD NOT be deployed in networks that do not deploy ingress filtering [BCP38] and also have inadequately secured UDP endpoints. | Deployment guidance for servers only; N/A for the client library. |
| `21.2-21.14-12` | MUST | Any future extension that allows server migration must also define countermeasures for request forgery attacks. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Any future extension that allows server migration MUST also define countermeasures for forgery attacks. | Constrains future spec authors; client implements no migration — nothing to check. |
| `21.2-21.14-13` | informative | Clients are not obligated to use tokens received in NEW_TOKEN frames. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | Clients, however, are not obligated to use the NEW_TOKEN frame. | quic/initial.go: client sends an empty Token field on fresh connections (no NEW_TOKEN reuse) — confirm and document as compliant. |
| `21.2-21.14-14` | informative | Token-field request forgery is avoided if a client sends an empty Token field whenever the server address differs from the address at which the NEW_TOKEN frame was received. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | clients send an empty Token field when the server address has changed from when the NEW_TOKEN frame was received | quic/frame.go (NEW_TOKEN parse) + initial.go: if NEW_TOKEN tokens are ever stored for reuse, bind them to the server address and discard on change; if NEW_TOKEN is ignored, trivially compliant. |
| `21.2-21.14-15` | MUST NOT | A client must not send non-probing frames to a server's preferred address before it has validated that address. <br>_(client [CLIENT])_ | A client MUST NOT send non-probing frames to a preferred address prior to validating that address | quic/transport_params.go: verify preferred_address is parsed/skipped and the client never migrates to it (no-migration design makes this trivially satisfied; guard needed if migration is ever added). |
| `21.2-21.14-16` | MAY | Endpoints may block connection attempts or migration to a loopback address as a forgery countermeasure. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY prevent connection attempts or migration to a loopback address. | http3.Dial / quic dial path: discretionary loopback filtering not implemented — acceptable for a load generator that dials caller-supplied addresses; document as deliberate. |
| `21.2-21.14-17` | SHOULD NOT | Endpoints should not allow connections or migration to a loopback address when the same service was previously reachable at a different interface or the address came from a non-loopback service. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints SHOULD NOT allow connections or migration to a loopback address if the same service was previously available at a different interface | quic/transport_params.go + client dial: no server-directed re-addressing exists (preferred_address unused, no migration), so the risky redirect path is unreachable — confirm and note. |
| `21.2-21.14-18` | SHOULD NOT | Endpoints should not refuse to use an address (e.g., link-local or private-range) unless they have specific network knowledge that sending to unvalidated addresses in that range is unsafe. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints SHOULD NOT refuse to use an address unless they have specific knowledge about the network indicating that sending datagrams to unvalidated addresses in a given range is not safe | Client dial performs no address-range filtering — compliant by default; keep it that way unless a caller-opt-in policy is added. |
| `21.2-21.14-19` | MAY | Endpoints may reduce request-forgery risk by omitting NEW_TOKEN token values from Initial packets or by sending only probing frames before address validation completes. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY choose to reduce the risk of request forgery by not including values from NEW_TOKEN frames in Initial packets | quic/initial.go: empty-token default already satisfies the first option; the probing-frames option is moot without migration. |
| `21.2-21.14-20` | MAY | Endpoints may avoid sending datagrams to UDP ports known as attack targets, or datagrams matching known attack patterns, before the destination address is validated. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY choose to avoid sending datagrams to these ports or not send datagrams that match these patterns prior to validating the destination address | quic/send.go: discretionary port/pattern filtering not implemented — acceptable; note only. |
| `21.2-21.14-21` | MAY | Endpoints may retire peer-issued connection IDs containing problematic byte patterns without ever using them. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY retire connection IDs containing patterns known to be problematic without using them. | quic/connid.go: verify the client is capable of sending RETIRE_CONNECTION_ID for a server-issued CID it chooses not to use (capability check only; policy is discretionary). |
| `21.2-21.14-22` | SHOULD | QUIC deployments should mitigate Slowloris-style attacks (connection caps per IP, minimum transfer speed, maximum connection lifetime, etc.). <br>_(server,endpoint [not-client])_ | QUIC deployments SHOULD provide mitigations for the Slowloris attacks | Server-victim attack class; N/A for a client-only load generator (the listed mitigations are all server knobs). |
| `21.2-21.14-23` | SHOULD | QUIC deployments should mitigate stream fragmentation and reassembly attacks, e.g., by avoiding memory overcommitment, bounding tracking structures, delaying reassembly, or hole age/duration heuristics. <br>_(endpoint,receiver [CLIENT])_ | QUIC deployments SHOULD provide mitigations for stream fragmentation attacks. | quic/stream.go receive reassembly + recvflow.go: verify reassembly-gap tracking structures are bounded and retained memory is charged against (and capped by) the advertised flow-control window — matches the repo peer-input policy. |
| `21.2-21.14-24` | informative | The receiver-side fragmentation attack is mitigated when advertised flow control windows correspond to actually available memory (overcommitting windows renders the endpoint vulnerable). <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | The attack on receivers is mitigated if flow control windows correspond to available memory. | quic/recvflow.go: verify MAX_DATA / MAX_STREAM_DATA advertisements do not overcommit relative to what the client is actually prepared to buffer. |
| `21.2-21.14-25` | informative | Receiving a stream frame for a higher-numbered stream ID obligates the receiver to open all intervening streams of the same type, which an attacker can exploit to exhaust state. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | On receiving a higher-numbered stream ID, a receiver is required to open all intervening streams of the same type | quic/stream.go: verify the server-opened-stream path enforces advertised MAX_STREAMS limits before allocating, and that per-stream state for implicitly opened intervening streams is minimal. |
| `21.2-21.14-26` | informative | The number of active peer-opened streams is bounded by the initial_max_streams_bidi/initial_max_streams_uni transport parameters as raised by MAX_STREAMS frames; judicious limits mitigate the stream commitment attack. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The number of active streams is limited by the initial_max_streams_bidi and initial_max_streams_uni transport parameters as updated by any received MAX_STREAMS frames | quic/transport_params.go + recvflow.go: verify the client advertises finite, modest initial_max_streams_uni/bidi (HTTP/3 needs only ~3 server uni streams) so server-opened stream state is bounded. |
| `21.2-21.14-27` | SHOULD | Implementations should track processing cost relative to connection progress and treat excessive quantities of non-productive packets (e.g., tiny flow-control increments, state-churn frames) as an attack. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | implementations SHOULD track cost of processing relative to progress and treat excessive quantities of any non-productive packets as indicative of an attack | quic connection read loop (conn/frame dispatch in quic/): no cost-vs-progress accounting exists — documented gap; low risk for a load generator dialing chosen servers, but consider counters for non-productive frames. |
| `21.2-21.14-28` | MAY | Endpoints may respond to detected peer denial-of-service (excessive non-productive packets) with a connection error or by dropping packets. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints MAY respond to this condition with a connection error or by dropping packets. | quic/close.go + errors.go: discretionary response path; only relevant if the SHOULD in 21.2-21.14-27 is ever implemented. |
| `21.2-21.14-29` | informative | Endpoints ignore the ECN field of an IP packet unless at least one QUIC packet in that IP packet is successfully processed, defeating spoofed/duplicated-ECN manipulation. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | QUIC endpoints ignore the ECN field in an IP packet unless at least one QUIC packet in that IP packet is successfully processed | quic/ack_tracker.go ECN counts + datagram receive path (incl. gro batching): verify ECN counts are incremented only for datagrams in which at least one packet decrypted successfully. |
| `21.2-21.14-30` | MUST | Server instances sharing a static stateless-reset key must be arranged so packets for a given connection ID always reach an instance holding that connection's state while it is active. <br>_(endpoint,server [not-client])_ | MUST be arranged so that packets with a given connection ID always arrive at an instance that has connection state, unless that connection is no longer active | Server-cluster deployment rule; the client never generates stateless resets (out of scope) — confirm quic/ has no reset-generation code. |
| `21.2-21.14-31` | MUST NOT | A server must not generate a stateless reset when a connection with the corresponding connection ID could still be active on any endpoint sharing the same static key. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | servers MUST NOT generate a stateless reset if a connection with the corresponding connection ID could be active on any endpoint using the same static key | Server-only; client-only quic/ generates no stateless resets — no action. |
| `21.2-21.14-32` | informative | Sending a stateless reset instead of waiting for timeout after a load-balancer reconfiguration is acceptable only when routing cannot be influenced by an attacker. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | this is acceptable only if the routing cannot be influenced by an attacker | Server-deployment guidance; N/A for the client. |
| `21.2-21.14-33` | MUST | Future QUIC versions that use Version Negotiation packets must define a mechanism robust against version downgrade attacks (v1 VN itself has none). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Future versions of QUIC that use Version Negotiation packets MUST define a mechanism that is robust against version downgrade attacks. | Constrains future specs; quic/packet.go is v1-only and should abort on Version Negotiation rather than downgrade — nothing further to check. |
| `21.2-21.14-34` | informative | Deployments should make routing decisions for new connections independently of client-selected values, including addresses, to prevent targeted instance attacks. <br>_(server [not-client])_ | Ideally, routing decisions are made independently of client-selected values, including addresses. | Server load-balancing guidance; N/A for the client. |
| `21.2-21.14-35` | informative | The PADDING frame exists so endpoints can obscure the true length of packet content against traffic analysis; its use for this purpose is discretionary. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The PADDING frame is provided so that endpoints have some ability to obscure the length of packet content | quic/frame_write.go: PADDING emission exists (used for Initial datagram padding); traffic-analysis padding beyond that is discretionary and unimplemented — note only. |

### 9000 section 22 - IANA Considerations (registration policies, QUIC Versions / Transport Parameters / Frame Types / Transport Error Codes registries)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `22-1` | informative | All QUIC registries support both provisional and permanent codepoint registrations under a common set of policies. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | All QUIC registries allow for both provisional and permanent registration of codepoints. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-2` | informative | Provisional registrations require Expert Review, and experts may reject only requests for an excessive proportion of remaining space or the very first unassigned value. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Provisional registrations require Expert Review, as defined in Section 4.5 of [RFC8126]. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-3` | MAY | Provisional registrations may omit the Specification and Notes fields and any additional permanent-only fields. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Provisional registrations MAY omit the Specification and Notes fields, plus any additional fields that might be required for a permanent registration. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-4` | informative | Every QUIC registry carries the fields Value, Status (permanent/provisional), Specification, Date, Change Controller, Contact, and Notes. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | All QUIC registries include the following fields to support provisional registration: | No code impact — IANA registry schema only. |
| `22-5` | SHOULD | New codepoint requests should use a randomly selected codepoint that avoids existing allocations and the first unallocated codepoint. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | New requests for codepoints from QUIC registries SHOULD use a randomly selected codepoint that excludes both existing allocations and the first unallocated codepoint in the selected space. | No code impact unless the project registers extension codepoints (none in quic/). |
| `22-6` | MAY | Requests for multiple codepoints may use a contiguous range. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Requests for multiple codepoints MAY use a contiguous range. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-7` | informative | The first unassigned codepoint in each space is reserved for allocation via the Standards Action policy. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The use of the first unassigned codepoint is reserved for allocation using the Standards Action policy | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-8` | SHOULD | For varint-encoded codepoints (e.g. frame types), new registrations should use values 2^14 and above (4- or 8-byte encodings) unless a short encoding is essential. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | codepoints that encode to four or eight bytes (that is, values 2^14 and above) SHOULD be used unless the usage is especially sensitive to having a longer encoding. | No code impact for QUIC v1; note quic/frame.go must already parse varint frame types of any length (see 22-48). |
| `22-9` | MAY | A registration application may include a specific requested codepoint. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Applications to register codepoints in QUIC registries MAY include a requested codepoint as part of the registration. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-10` | MUST | IANA must allocate a requested codepoint if it is unassigned and the registration policy requirements are met. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | IANA MUST allocate the selected codepoint if the codepoint is unassigned and the requirements of the registration policy are met. | No code impact — obligation on IANA only. |
| `22-11` | SHOULD | Reclaiming provisional codepoints should target only the entries with the earliest recorded date. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | This SHOULD be done only for the codepoints with the earliest recorded date | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-12` | SHOULD NOT | Provisional entries updated less than a year prior should not be reclaimed. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | entries that have been updated less than a year prior SHOULD NOT be reclaimed | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-13` | MUST | A request to remove a codepoint must be reviewed by the designated experts. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | A request to remove a codepoint MUST be reviewed by the designated experts. | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-14` | MUST | The designated experts must attempt to determine whether a codepoint proposed for removal is still in use. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The experts MUST attempt to determine whether the codepoint is still in use. | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-15` | MUST NOT | A codepoint must not be reclaimed if any use is identified or an update request is made; the registration date is updated instead. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | If any use of the codepoints is identified by this search or a request to update the registration is made, the codepoint MUST NOT be reclaimed. | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-16` | MAY | A codepoint with no identified use and no update request may be removed from the registry. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | If no use of the codepoint was identified and no request was made to update the registration, the codepoint MAY be removed from the registry. | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-17` | informative | The same review-and-consultation process applies when converting a provisional registration to permanent, aimed at confirming the registration accurately reflects deployed usage. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | This review and consultation process also applies to requests to change a provisional registration into a permanent registration | No code impact — IANA registry maintenance only. |
| `22-18` | informative | Permanent registrations use the Specification Required policy unless otherwise specified, with experts biased toward approval unless abusive, frivolous, or actively harmful. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Permanent registrations in QUIC registries use the Specification Required policy (Section 4.6 of [RFC8126]), unless otherwise specified. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-19` | MAY | A registry's creation may specify additional constraints on permanent registrations. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The creation of a registry MAY specify additional constraints on permanent registrations. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-20` | MAY | A registry's creation may identify a range of codepoints governed by a different registration policy (e.g. QUIC Frame Types values 0-63). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | The creation of a registry MAY identify a range of codepoints where registrations are governed by a different registration policy. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-21` | informative | Stricter permanent-registration requirements do not prevent provisional registrations for the same codepoints. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | Any stricter requirements for permanent registrations do not prevent provisional registrations for affected codepoints. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-22` | MUST | All registrations made by Standards Track publications must be permanent. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | All registrations made by Standards Track publications MUST be permanent. | No code impact — IANA registration procedure only. |
| `22-23` | informative | All registrations made by RFC 9000 itself are permanent, with the IETF as change controller and the QUIC WG as contact. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | All registrations in this document are assigned a permanent status and list a change controller of the IETF | No code impact — IANA registry contents only. |
| `22-24` | informative | The QUIC Versions registry governs a 32-bit space (the Version field), with permanent registrations under Specification Required. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The "QUIC Versions" registry governs a 32-bit space; see Section 15. | quic/packet.go — Version field in long headers is a 32-bit big-endian value; confirm uint32 encode/decode. |
| `22-25` | informative | Codepoint 0x00000001 is permanently assigned as the version number of the QUIC v1 protocol defined by RFC 9000. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The codepoint of 0x00000001 for the protocol is assigned with permanent status to the protocol defined in this document. | quic/packet.go — verify the QUIC v1 version constant is exactly 0x00000001 in Initial encode and all long-header parse paths. |
| `22-26` | informative | Codepoint 0x00000000 is permanently reserved and denotes version negotiation, not a real protocol version. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The codepoint of 0x00000000 is permanently reserved; the note for this codepoint indicates that this version is reserved for version negotiation. | quic/packet.go — Version==0 on receive must be parsed as a Version Negotiation packet; client must never emit version 0 in its own packets. |
| `22-27` | MUST NOT | All version codepoints matching the pattern 0x?a?a?a?a are reserved (for forcing version negotiation / grease) and are never assigned or listed by IANA. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | All codepoints that follow the pattern 0x?a?a?a?a are reserved, MUST NOT be assigned by IANA, and MUST NOT appear in the listing of assigned values. | quic/packet.go — when processing a Version Negotiation packet, the client must never select a 0x?a?a?a?a value as a usable version. |
| `22-28` | informative | The QUIC Transport Parameters registry governs a 62-bit space (parameter IDs are varints), with values 0x00-0x3f under Standards Action or IESG Approval. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The "QUIC Transport Parameters" registry governs a 62-bit space. | quic/transport_params.go — TP IDs must be decoded as full 62-bit varints; unknown IDs of any magnitude skipped by their length field without error. |
| `22-29` | MUST | Permanent transport-parameter registrations must include a Parameter Name field (a short mnemonic). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | permanent registrations in this registry MUST include the following field: Parameter Name: A short mnemonic for the parameter. | No code impact — IANA registry schema only. |
| `22-30` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x00 is original_destination_connection_id (server-only, defined in Section 18.2). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x00 \| original_destination_connection_id | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x00 maps to original_destination_connection_id; client decodes and validates it against the Initial DCID but never sends it. |
| `22-31` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x01 is max_idle_timeout. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x01 \| max_idle_timeout | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x01 maps to max_idle_timeout (milliseconds, varint). |
| `22-32` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x02 is stateless_reset_token (server-only). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x02 \| stateless_reset_token | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x02 maps to stateless_reset_token; client accepts from server (16 bytes) but must not send it. |
| `22-33` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x03 is max_udp_payload_size. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x03 \| max_udp_payload_size | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x03 maps to max_udp_payload_size; quic/send.go + initial.go respect the peer's value when sizing datagrams. |
| `22-34` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x04 is initial_max_data. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x04 \| initial_max_data | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x04 maps to initial_max_data; seeds connection-level send limit consumed by quic/recvflow.go peers. |
| `22-35` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x05 is initial_max_stream_data_bidi_local. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x05 \| initial_max_stream_data_bidi_local | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x05 maps to initial_max_stream_data_bidi_local; quic/stream.go applies it to the correct stream direction (streams the TP sender opens). |
| `22-36` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x06 is initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x06 \| initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x06 maps to initial_max_stream_data_bidi_remote; quic/stream.go applies it to peer-initiated bidi streams. |
| `22-37` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x07 is initial_max_stream_data_uni. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x07 \| initial_max_stream_data_uni | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x07 maps to initial_max_stream_data_uni; quic/stream.go applies it to unidirectional streams. |
| `22-38` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x08 is initial_max_streams_bidi. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x08 \| initial_max_streams_bidi | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x08 maps to initial_max_streams_bidi; seeds the bidi stream-count limit tracked in quic/recvflow.go. |
| `22-39` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x09 is initial_max_streams_uni. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x09 \| initial_max_streams_uni | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x09 maps to initial_max_streams_uni; seeds the uni stream-count limit in quic/recvflow.go. |
| `22-40` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0a is ack_delay_exponent. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0a \| ack_delay_exponent | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x0a maps to ack_delay_exponent; quic/ack_tracker.go must scale received ACK Delay fields by the peer's exponent (default 3). |
| `22-41` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0b is max_ack_delay. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0b \| max_ack_delay | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x0b maps to max_ack_delay; loss-recovery PTO computation uses the peer's value. |
| `22-42` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0c is disable_active_migration. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0c \| disable_active_migration | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x0c maps to disable_active_migration (zero-length); client never migrates, so receipt is a no-op but must parse. |
| `22-43` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0d is preferred_address (server-only). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0d \| preferred_address | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x0d maps to preferred_address; client must parse/skip the compound value without error and must never send it. |
| `22-44` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0e is active_connection_id_limit. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0e \| active_connection_id_limit | quic/transport_params.go + quic/connid.go — confirm ID 0x0e maps to active_connection_id_limit (min 2, default 2) and NEW_CONNECTION_ID handling honors it. |
| `22-45` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x0f is initial_source_connection_id. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0f \| initial_source_connection_id | quic/transport_params.go — confirm ID 0x0f maps to initial_source_connection_id; client sends its own and validates the server's against the handshake SCID. |
| `22-46` | informative | Transport parameter codepoint 0x10 is retry_source_connection_id (server-only). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x10 \| retry_source_connection_id | quic/transport_params.go + quic/retry.go — confirm ID 0x10 maps to retry_source_connection_id; client validates it after a Retry and must never send it. |
| `22-47` | MUST NOT | Every transport parameter value of the form 31*N+27 (27, 58, 89, ...) is reserved for grease and is never assigned or listed by IANA. <br>_(all [CLIENT])_ | Each value of the form "31 * N + 27" for integer values of N (that is, 27, 58, 89, ...) are reserved; these values MUST NOT be assigned by IANA | quic/transport_params.go — decoder must silently skip reserved IDs 31N+27 (they will appear from greasing servers); if the client greases, it picks IDs from this set. |
| `22-48` | informative | The QUIC Frame Types registry governs a 62-bit space (frame types are varints), with values 0x00-0x3f under Standards Action or IESG Approval. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The "QUIC Frame Types" registry governs a 62-bit space. | quic/frame.go — frame type must be read as a full varint (up to 8 bytes); an unknown 62-bit type is classified as unknown (FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR per Section 12.4), not truncated to one byte. |
| `22-49` | MUST | Permanent frame-type registrations must include a Frame Type Name field (a short mnemonic). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | permanent registrations in this registry MUST include the following field: Frame Type Name: A short mnemonic for the frame type. | No code impact — IANA registry schema only. |
| `22-50` | SHOULD | Specifications for new frame types should describe how an endpoint determines it may send that frame (usually via an accompanying transport parameter). <br>_(all [not-client])_ | specifications for new permanent registrations SHOULD describe the means by which an endpoint might determine that it can send the identified type of frame. | No code impact for QUIC v1; relevant only when adding extension frames (e.g. DATAGRAM) gated on a TP in quic/transport_params.go. |
| `22-51` | informative | The initial Frame Types registry contents are the frame-type codepoints of Table 3 (0x00-0x1e), without the Pkts and Spec columns. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The initial contents of this registry are tabulated in Table 3. | quic/frame.go — frame-type constants must match Table 3 values 0x00 PADDING through 0x1e HANDSHAKE_DONE (covered in the Section 19 extraction). |
| `22-52` | informative | The QUIC Transport Error Codes registry governs a 62-bit space split into three policy ranges, so error codes are full varints. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | The "QUIC Transport Error Codes" registry governs a 62-bit space. This space is split into three ranges that are governed by different policies. | quic/errors.go + quic/close.go — error-code fields in CONNECTION_CLOSE are varints; store as uint64 and accept any 62-bit value from the peer without rejecting unknown codes. |
| `22-53` | MUST | Permanent error-code registrations must include Code (mnemonic) and Description fields; the Description may be a summary when a specification is referenced. <br>_(all [not-client])_ | permanent registrations in this registry MUST include the following fields: Code: A short mnemonic for the parameter. | No code impact — IANA registry schema only. |
| `22-54` | informative | Transport error code 0x00 is NO_ERROR (no error). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x00 \| NO_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm NO_ERROR == 0x00; graceful close in quic/close.go uses it. |
| `22-55` | informative | Transport error code 0x01 is INTERNAL_ERROR (implementation error). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x01 \| INTERNAL_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm INTERNAL_ERROR == 0x01. |
| `22-56` | informative | Transport error code 0x02 is CONNECTION_REFUSED (server refuses a connection). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x02 \| CONNECTION_REFUSED | quic/errors.go — confirm CONNECTION_REFUSED == 0x02; client only receives this (from server), surfaces it as a dial failure. |
| `22-57` | informative | Transport error code 0x03 is FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR (flow control violated). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x03 \| FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm FLOW_CONTROL_ERROR == 0x03; quic/recvflow.go and stream.go raise it when peer data exceeds advertised limits. |
| `22-58` | informative | Transport error code 0x04 is STREAM_LIMIT_ERROR (too many streams opened). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x04 \| STREAM_LIMIT_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm STREAM_LIMIT_ERROR == 0x04; raised when peer opens a stream beyond MAX_STREAMS. |
| `22-59` | informative | Transport error code 0x05 is STREAM_STATE_ERROR (frame received in invalid stream state). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x05 \| STREAM_STATE_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm STREAM_STATE_ERROR == 0x05; quic/stream.go raises it for state-machine violations (e.g. STOP_SENDING on an unopened receive-only stream). |
| `22-60` | informative | Transport error code 0x06 is FINAL_SIZE_ERROR (change to a stream's final size). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x06 \| FINAL_SIZE_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm FINAL_SIZE_ERROR == 0x06; quic/stream.go raises it on FIN/RESET_STREAM final-size inconsistencies. |
| `22-61` | informative | Transport error code 0x07 is FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR (malformed or unknown frame encoding). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x07 \| FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm FRAME_ENCODING_ERROR == 0x07; quic/frame.go parse failures and unknown frame types map to it. |
| `22-62` | informative | Transport error code 0x08 is TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR (error in transport parameters). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x08 \| TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm TRANSPORT_PARAMETER_ERROR == 0x08; quic/transport_params.go validation failures (duplicates, bad lengths, client-forbidden params) map to it. |
| `22-63` | informative | Transport error code 0x09 is CONNECTION_ID_LIMIT_ERROR (too many connection IDs received). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x09 \| CONNECTION_ID_LIMIT_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm CONNECTION_ID_LIMIT_ERROR == 0x09; quic/connid.go raises it when NEW_CONNECTION_ID exceeds active_connection_id_limit. |
| `22-64` | informative | Transport error code 0x0a is PROTOCOL_VIOLATION (generic protocol violation). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0a \| PROTOCOL_VIOLATION | quic/errors.go — confirm PROTOCOL_VIOLATION == 0x0a; the generic fallback for violations without a more specific code. |
| `22-65` | informative | Transport error code 0x0b is INVALID_TOKEN (invalid token received). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0b \| INVALID_TOKEN | quic/errors.go — confirm INVALID_TOKEN == 0x0b; client only receives it (server rejects the client's Initial token) and surfaces it as a connection error. |
| `22-66` | informative | Transport error code 0x0c is APPLICATION_ERROR (application-layer error). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0c \| APPLICATION_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm APPLICATION_ERROR == 0x0c; used when an application close (0x1d) must be signaled in a 0x1c CONNECTION_CLOSE during handshake (quic/close.go). |
| `22-67` | informative | Transport error code 0x0d is CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED (CRYPTO data buffer overflowed). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0d \| CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED | quic/errors.go — confirm CRYPTO_BUFFER_EXCEEDED == 0x0d; raised when buffered out-of-order CRYPTO data exceeds the implementation's limit. |
| `22-68` | informative | Transport error code 0x0e is KEY_UPDATE_ERROR (invalid packet protection update). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0e \| KEY_UPDATE_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm KEY_UPDATE_ERROR == 0x0e; key-update violation handling (RFC 9001) must use this code. |
| `22-69` | informative | Transport error code 0x0f is AEAD_LIMIT_REACHED (excessive use of packet protection keys). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0f \| AEAD_LIMIT_REACHED | quic/errors.go — confirm AEAD_LIMIT_REACHED == 0x0f; used when AEAD confidentiality/integrity limits are hit before a key update is possible. |
| `22-70` | informative | Transport error code 0x10 is NO_VIABLE_PATH (no network path capable of supporting minimum QUIC requirements exists). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x10 \| NO_VIABLE_PATH | quic/errors.go — confirm NO_VIABLE_PATH == 0x10; relevant to quic/initial.go PMTU handling if the path cannot carry 1200-byte datagrams. |
| `22-71` | informative | Transport error codes 0x0100-0x01ff are CRYPTO_ERROR, carrying a TLS alert code as 0x0100 plus the alert value. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | 0x0100-\| CRYPTO_ERROR | quic/errors.go — confirm CRYPTO_ERROR is a 256-value range with base 0x0100; TLS handshake alerts must be sent/decoded as 0x0100\|alert, not a single constant. |

### 9000 section A - Pseudocode (varint decoding, packet number encoding/decoding, ECN validation)

| ID | Level | Requirement (scope) | RFC 9000 verbatim | Impl check / status |
|----|-------|---------------------|-------------------|---------------------|
| `A-1` | informative | Appendix A algorithms are samples: correct and clear reference behaviour, not mandatory implementations and not optimized for performance. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | These algorithms are intended to be correct and clear, rather than being optimally performant. | quic/ + internal/bytesx: implementations may differ from the pseudocode as long as observable results match; use these as reference oracles in tests. |
| `A-2` | informative | Variable-length integers are read from a byte sequence in network byte order (big-endian). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | a sequence of bytes, which can be read in network byte order | internal/bytesx: confirm varint decoder accumulates bytes big-endian. |
| `A-3` | informative | Varint length is derived from the first byte: prefix = first_byte >> 6, and total encoded length in bytes = 1 << prefix (1, 2, 4, or 8). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | v = data.next_byte() prefix = v >> 6 length = 1 << prefix | internal/bytesx: verify decoder computes length as 1<<(b>>6) before consuming further bytes and errors on short input. |
| `A-4` | informative | After determining length, the decoder masks the two prefix bits (v & 0x3f) and folds in the remaining length-1 bytes via v = (v << 8) + next_byte. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | v = v & 0x3f repeat length-1 times: v = (v << 8) + data.next_byte() return v | internal/bytesx: verify the 0x3f mask on the first byte and shift-accumulate loop match this exactly. |
| `A-5` | informative | Varint test vectors: 0xc2197c5eff14e88c (8 bytes) decodes to 151,288,809,941,952,652 and 0x9d7f3e7d (4 bytes) decodes to 494,878,333. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | the eight-byte sequence 0xc2197c5eff14e88c decodes to the decimal value 151,288,809,941,952,652; the four-byte sequence 0x9d7f3e7d decodes to 494,878,333 | internal/bytesx: ensure unit tests include both 8-byte and 4-byte RFC vectors. |
| `A-6` | informative | Varint test vectors: 0x7bbd (2 bytes) decodes to 15,293; 0x25 (1 byte) decodes to 37; the non-minimal 2-byte encoding 0x4025 also decodes to 37. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | the two-byte sequence 0x7bbd decodes to 15,293; and the single byte 0x25 decodes to 37 (as does the two-byte sequence 0x4025) | internal/bytesx: decoder must accept non-minimal encodings generically (0x4025 -> 37); only frame-type fields have a separate minimal-encoding rule (RFC 9000 §12.4, enforced in frame.go, not the varint codec). |
| `A-7` | informative | The packet number encoding size must use at least one bit more than log2 of the number of contiguous unacknowledged packet numbers including the new packet (normative MUST lives in §17.1; restated here as the algorithm's invariant). <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | The number of bits must be at least one more than the base-2 logarithm of the number of contiguous unacknowledged packet numbers, including the new packet. | quic/packet_write.go: verify chosen packet-number length satisfies this bound for every send; cross-check against the §17.1 MUST fact from part 1. |
| `A-8` | informative | EncodePacketNumber computes num_unacked = full_pn + 1 when nothing has been acknowledged yet, else full_pn - largest_acked. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | if largest_acked is None: num_unacked = full_pn + 1 else: num_unacked = full_pn - largest_acked | quic/packet_write.go: check the no-ack-yet branch uses full_pn+1, not a fixed minimum length. |
| `A-9` | informative | EncodePacketNumber sizes the field as num_bytes = ceil((log2(num_unacked) + 1) / 8), yielding 1-4 bytes. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | min_bits = log(num_unacked, 2) + 1 num_bytes = ceil(min_bits / 8) | quic/packet_write.go: verify byte-count computation (typically via bits.Len) matches ceil((log2+1)/8) at power-of-two boundaries. |
| `A-10` | informative | The encoded packet number is the full packet number truncated to its num_bytes least significant bytes. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | Encode the integer value and truncate to the num_bytes least significant bytes. | quic/packet_write.go: confirm truncation keeps the low-order bytes and the header's two PN-length bits equal num_bytes-1. |
| `A-11` | informative | Encoding vector: with largest_acked 0xabe8b3 and full_pn 0xac5c02 there are 29,519 outstanding numbers, so representing twice that range (59,038) needs 16 bits (2-byte encoding). <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | there are 29,519 (0x734f) outstanding packet numbers. In order to represent at least twice this range (59,038 packets, or 0xe69e), 16 bits are required | quic/packet_write.go tests: add this RFC vector (0xabe8b3 acked, send 0xac5c02 -> 2-byte PN). |
| `A-12` | informative | Encoding vector: in the same state, full_pn 0xace8fe requires 18 bits to cover twice the range (131,222), so the 24-bit (3-byte) encoding is used. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | sending a packet with a number of 0xace8fe uses the 24-bit encoding, because at least 18 bits are required to represent twice the range (131,222 packets, or 0x020096) | quic/packet_write.go tests: add this vector (0xabe8b3 acked, send 0xace8fe -> 3-byte PN) to pin the rounding-up-to-whole-bytes behaviour. |
| `A-13` | informative | Packet number decoding runs on the truncated Packet Number field only after header protection has been removed. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | decoding packet numbers after header protection has been removed | quic/packet.go: confirm decode ordering — header-protection removal first, then PN reconstruction, then AEAD with the reconstructed PN. |
| `A-14` | informative | DecodePacketNumber inputs are largest_pn (largest successfully processed in the space), truncated_pn, and pn_nbits which is exactly 8, 16, 24, or 32. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | pn_nbits is the number of bits in the Packet Number field (8, 16, 24, or 32) | quic/packet.go: decoder must handle all four field widths and track largest_pn per packet-number space (Initial/Handshake/1-RTT separately). |
| `A-15` | informative | The decode window is built as expected_pn = largest_pn + 1, pn_win = 1 << pn_nbits, pn_hwin = pn_win / 2, pn_mask = pn_win - 1. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | expected_pn = largest_pn + 1 pn_win = 1 << pn_nbits pn_hwin = pn_win / 2 pn_mask = pn_win - 1 | quic/packet.go: verify window constants derive from the received field width, not a fixed 32-bit assumption. |
| `A-16` | informative | The reconstructed packet number should fall in the window (expected_pn - pn_hwin, expected_pn + pn_hwin]; simply splicing truncated bits onto expected_pn can land outside it. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | The incoming packet number should be greater than expected_pn - pn_hwin and less than or equal to expected_pn + pn_hwin | quic/packet.go: check window is half-open exactly as stated (strictly greater on the low side, inclusive on the high side). |
| `A-17` | informative | The candidate value is formed by replacing the low bits of expected_pn with the truncated field: candidate_pn = (expected_pn & ~pn_mask) \| truncated_pn. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | candidate_pn = (expected_pn & ~pn_mask) \| truncated_pn | quic/packet.go: verify candidate construction masks against expected_pn (largest_pn+1), not largest_pn itself. |
| `A-18` | informative | If the candidate is at or below the low edge of the window, add pn_win — but only when candidate_pn < 2^62 - pn_win, guarding against overflowing the 2^62-1 packet number ceiling. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | if candidate_pn <= expected_pn - pn_hwin and candidate_pn < (1 << 62) - pn_win: return candidate_pn + pn_win | quic/packet.go: confirm the (1<<62)-pn_win overflow guard exists; without it a hostile truncated PN near the ceiling produces an out-of-range number. |
| `A-19` | informative | If the candidate is above the high edge of the window, subtract pn_win — but only when candidate_pn >= pn_win (underflow guard); otherwise return the candidate unchanged. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | if candidate_pn > expected_pn + pn_hwin and candidate_pn >= pn_win: return candidate_pn - pn_win | quic/packet.go: confirm the candidate_pn >= pn_win underflow guard and the fall-through return of the raw candidate. |
| `A-20` | informative | Decoding vector: with largest authenticated packet number 0xa82f30ea, a 16-bit truncated value 0x9b32 decodes to 0xa82f9b32. <br>_(receiver [CLIENT])_ | a packet containing a 16-bit value of 0x9b32 will be decoded as 0xa82f9b32 | quic/packet.go tests: pin this RFC vector (largest 0xa82f30ea + 0x9b32/16-bit -> 0xa82f9b32). |
| `A-21` | informative | Whenever an endpoint starts sending on a new network path it determines whether the path supports ECN (per Section 13.4) before relying on ECN marking. <br>_(endpoint,sender [CLIENT])_ | Each time an endpoint commences sending on a new network path, it determines whether the path supports ECN | quic/send.go: client is single-path (no migration), so at most one validation at connection start; if the client never sets ECT on outgoing datagrams, A.4 is N/A on the send side — verify which. |
| `A-22` | informative | The ECN validation algorithm in A.4 is one sample method; endpoints are free to implement different path-testing methods. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | This algorithm is intended to show how a path might be tested for ECN support. Endpoints can implement different methods. | quic/send.go + ack_tracker.go: any ECN enablement logic need not match A.4 literally, but must satisfy the normative validation rules of §13.4.2 (part 1 facts). |
| `A-23` | informative | Sample state machine: path ECN state is one of testing/unknown/failed/capable; in testing or capable the endpoint sends ECT-marked packets (ECT(0) by default), otherwise unmarked packets. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | On paths with a "testing" or "capable" state, the endpoint sends packets with an ECT marking -- ECT(0) by default; otherwise, the endpoint sends unmarked packets. | quic/send.go + gro/gso path: if ECN sending is implemented, socket must set ECT(0) in the IP TOS/TCLASS only in testing/capable states; if not implemented, all datagrams stay unmarked (Not-ECT) — verify. |
| `A-24` | informative | Sample: testing starts by setting state to testing and recording current ECN counts as a baseline for later validation deltas. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | To start testing a path, the ECN state is set to "testing", and existing ECN counts are remembered as a baseline. | quic/ack_tracker.go: ECN count comparisons during validation must be against a remembered baseline, not absolute counts. |
| `A-25` | informative | Sample: the testing period should send enough marked packets to be conclusive; the suggested bound is ten packets or three times the PTO. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | Section 13.4.2 suggests limiting this to ten packets or three times the PTO. | quic/send.go: if ECN testing exists, cap the testing window at ~10 packets or 3xPTO per §13.4.2. |
| `A-26` | informative | Sample: after the testing period the path state becomes unknown; unmarked packets are then sent until validation succeeds. <br>_(sender [CLIENT])_ | After the testing period ends, the ECN state for the path becomes "unknown". | quic/send.go: in the unknown state the sample sends unmarked packets (only testing/capable send ECT); verify state transitions if implemented. |
| `A-27` | informative | Sample: from unknown, successful validation of ECN counts in an ACK frame moves the path to capable, but only if at least one marked packet was actually acknowledged. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | successful validation of the ECN counts in an ACK frame (see Section 13.4.2.1) causes the ECN state for the path to become "capable", unless no marked packet has been acknowledged | quic/ack_tracker.go: do not treat an ACK carrying no ECN evidence as proof of capability — a capable verdict requires an acked marked packet. |
| `A-28` | informative | Sample: any failure of ECN count validation immediately moves the path to failed (ECN disabled for the path). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | If validation of ECN counts fails at any time, the ECN state for the affected path becomes "failed". | quic/ack_tracker.go: validation failure must permanently stop ECT marking on the path; ties to the normative §13.4.2.1 checks from part 1. |
| `A-29` | informative | Sample: an endpoint can also declare ECN failed when all marked packets are declared lost or all are reported ECN-CE marked (path mangling/bleaching detection). <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | An endpoint can also mark the ECN state for a path as "failed" if marked packets are all declared lost or if they are all ECN-CE marked. | quic/ack_tracker.go + loss recovery: if ECN is implemented, loss of all testing-period marked packets or 100% CE marking should fail the path. |
| `A-30` | informative | Sample: endpoints may periodically re-test a path previously determined not to support ECN. <br>_(endpoint [CLIENT])_ | Endpoints might also periodically reassess a path that was determined to not support ECN. | quic/send.go: optional — re-probing a failed ECN path is allowed but not required; fine to omit in a client-only implementation. |
